and gives a dual expression for the aforementioned bias.

Proposition 4
.

Let
{\mathscr{P}}
and
{\mathscr{N}}
be two complementary subsets of
\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
of size
s
and
n-s
respectively. Let
\mathcal{C}
be an
[n,k]
-code such that
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathscr{P}}}
is of dimension
s
and let
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
be an
[s,{k_{\textup{aux}}}]
-code. We have for any
{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}

(12)

\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)=\frac{1}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\>\frac{1}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}\sum_{j=0}^{s}N_{i,j}K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)

where

\displaystyle N_{i,j}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\left|\left\{\left({\mathbf{r}},{\mathbf{c}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)\in({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})\times\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\>:\left|{\mathbf{r}}\right|=j\mbox{ and }\left|\left({\mathbf{r}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+{\mathbf{c}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right|=i\right\}\right|,

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\times{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\>:\>|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}|=w\mbox{ and }|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}|={t_{\textup{aux}}}\}

and where
{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s\times(n-s)}
is such that for any
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}
we have
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}={\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\intercal}
.

Proof.

This proposition is proved in Appendix
C
.
∎

5.2.
Intuition on How this Formula Allows to Estimate
\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|

As a preliminary remark, notice that
\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)
is the same for all
{\mathbf{x}}
belonging to a same coset of
{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}
and therefore only possibly allows to distinguish the values
{\mathbf{x}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}
. Second, observe that the expected value of
\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|
is
\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}}{2^{k-s}}\frac{\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}
so that we expect

\frac{1}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\;\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\approx\frac{1}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

Third, observe that Proposition
2
means in essence that the bias corresponding to
{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathscr{P}}
, namely

\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{\mathscr{P}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)

should be
\approx\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(t-u)}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}
,
i.e.
it corresponds roughly to the “first” pair
(i,j)

(where we range the values according to the product
K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)
) for which
N_{i,j}\neq 0
, namely

(i,j)=(u,t-u)
where the pair
({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathscr{P}},\mathbf{0})
is likely to be the only pair
({\mathbf{r}},{\mathbf{c}}^{\mathscr{N}})
in
({\mathbf{e}}_{\mathscr{P}}+{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})\times\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}
such that

\left|{\mathbf{r}}\right|=t-u
and
\left|\left({\mathbf{r}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+{\mathbf{c}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right|=u
(and therefore we likely have

N_{u,t-u}=1
). Therefore the behavior of the sum appearing in (
12
) is dominated by this first
term
N_{i,j}
which is non zero, namely
(i,j)=(u,t-u)
since we really have in this case that the corresponding term in the
sum is nothing but

\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(t-u)}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

This kind of phenomenon appears to be much more general than this: the
{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}
which give a high bias (and are therefore the ones we put in
{\mathscr{S}}
) are those for which there is an
N_{i,j}
which is unexpectedly non zero (and therefore most likely equal to
1
)
in the low values of
(i,j)
for which the term
K_{w}^{n-s}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)
can compete or even supersede the term
dominating in the expression (
12
) of the bias of
{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}
, namely
K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(t-u)

(we have ignored the common denominator
2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|
appearing in both sums). Similarly we expect that the bias of those

{\mathbf{x}}
is of order in this case

\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(j)}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

This intuition is formalized by Conjecture
1
what we make later on.

5.3.
Main Proposition

The key step of the analysis is to estimate the number of candidates, namely the size of
{\mathscr{S}}
(Instruction
3
of Algorithm
4
).
Provided that the bet (
|{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}|=u
) on the error is valid we expect that the secret vector
{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}
belongs to
{\mathscr{S}}
. But, as we will show in this section this set also contains some false positives, namely any element of
{\mathscr{S}}\setminus\{{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}\}
. Testing if an element of
{\mathscr{S}}
is a false positive (Algorithm
3
) will be of exponential cost. Estimating their number is therefore crucial to predict the complexity of our algorithm.
The following proposition bounds the expected number of candidates in a typical iteration of Algorithm
4
.

Proposition 5
.

Using Distribution
1
for
\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{e}}
and
{\mathbf{y}}
and given that our parameters verify Parameter Constraint
1
, that the number of computed
\mathsf{LPN}
samples is the total number of available
\mathsf{LPN}
samples, i.e.

{\mathscr{H}}=\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}
and under Conjecture
1
we have that the expected number of candidates per iteration is bounded by

(13)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\binom{s}{j}\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\right)+1

where

(14)

\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\left(i,j\right)\in\llbracket 0,n-s\rrbracket\times\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket,\;\left|\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right|\leqslant n^{3.2}\right\}.

The set
{\mathscr{S}}
of candidates is defined by

(15)

{\mathscr{S}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\textup{aux}}}\>:\widehat{f_{{\mathbf{y}},\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}},{{\mathbf{G}}_{\textup{aux}}}}}\left({\mathbf{s}}\right)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\>\widetilde{H}\right\},

where

(16)

\widetilde{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\qquad\mbox{and}\qquad\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

Remark 3
.

The additional constraint that
{\mathscr{H}}=\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}
is only here to simplify the proof. One could make a similar proposition without this constraint. In our instantiation of Algorithm
4
and with our optimal parameters this constraint is de-facto verified. Note also that
\widetilde{H}
appearing in the expression of the threshold is the expected number of available
\mathsf{LPN}
samples, namely
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|\right)
.

Distribution 1
.

•

{\mathscr{P}}
and
{\mathscr{N}}
are two fixed complementary subsets of
\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
of size
s
and
n-s
respectively.

•

The code
\mathcal{C}
of generator matrix
{\mathbf{G}}
is chosen uniformly at random among
[n,k]
linear codes which are such that
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathscr{P}}}
is of dimension
s
.

•

The code
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
of generator matrix
{{\mathbf{G}}_{\textup{aux}}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}\times s}
is chosen uniformly at random among the
[s,{k_{\textup{aux}}}]
-codes.

•

{\mathbf{e}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
is a fixed vector of
\mathcal{S}_{t}^{n}
,
{\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{C}
is a random codeword of
\mathcal{C}
and we define
{\mathbf{y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}{\mathbf{c}}+{\mathbf{e}}
.

Correctness of our algorithm is ensured by the following constraints.

Parameter Constraint 1
.

We suppose that the parameters
n,k,t,s,{k_{\textup{aux}}},{t_{\textup{aux}}},w,u
are such that there exists a constant
\alpha>0
that is such that

(17)

(i)\;\;\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\omega\mathopen{}\left(\frac{n^{\alpha+8}}{\delta^{2}}\right),\;(ii)\;\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{k}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right),\;(iii)\;\;\frac{\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right).

where,

\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

Remark 4
.

Note that these constraints are in fact, up to a polynomial factor, the minimal constraints required for our algorithm to work. Indeed, there are precisely the constraints required in Proposition
2
which estimates the bias of the error of the
\mathsf{LPN}
samples (
5
).

The difficulty of proving Proposition
5
is similar to the difficulties encountered in analyzing the
\mathsf{RLPN}
algorithm in
[
MT23
]
, we know too little about the tails of the distribution of
N_{i,j}
. As such, we will make the following conjecture which formalizes the discussion in §
5.2
.

Conjecture 1
.

Using Distribution
1
, under Parameter Constraint
1
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)\\
=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i,j}\neq 0\right)+2^{-n}\right)

where
\mathcal{A}
is given in Equation (
14
) and
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
.

Conjecture
1
is discussed in the following section where we give experimental evidences that our analysis holds. In Appendix
F.1
we show that this conjecture is in fact a consequence of a more minimalistic conjecture.

6.
Experimental Evidence for Our Analysis

The goal of this section is to provide experimental evidence supporting Proposition
5
.
We will propose a convenient probabilistic model for the
N_{i,j}
’s and show that this model does not change the output distribution of our algorithm. We will essentially use the same model as in
[
MT23
, Appendix D]
and model the weight distribution of the coset of a random linear code as a Poisson distribution of the right expected value. Recall that
N_{i,j}
can be written as

N_{i,j}=\sum_{u=0}^{N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)}N_{i}\left(\left({\mathbf{r}}^{(u)}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)

where
{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s\times(n-s)}
is such that for any
\mathbf{h}\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}
we have
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}={\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\intercal}
,
{\mathbf{r}}^{(u)}
is the
u
’th codeword of weight
j
of
{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}
and
N_{j}({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}})
counts the number of elements in
{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}
of Hamming weight
j
. With our model, we first draw
N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)
according to a Poisson distribution of expected value
\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}
and then we draw each
N_{i}\left(\left({\mathbf{r}}^{(u)}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)
according to independent Poisson distributions of expected values
\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}

(see appendix
E
, Lemma
9
where we compute
\mathbb{E}\left(N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)\right)
and
\mathbb{E}\left(N_{i}\left(\left({\mathbf{r}}^{(u)}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)\right)
under Distribution
1
).
Finally, we get the following model for
N_{i,j}
by using the fact that the sum of independent Poisson random variables is a Poisson random variable:

Model 1

(Poisson Model)
.

Under Distribution
1
and when
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
we make the model that

N_{i,j}\sim\mathrm{Poisson}\left(N_{j}\>\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\right)\mbox{, where }N_{j}\sim\mathrm{Poisson}\left(\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right).

Under Poisson Model
1
, the following proposition proves Conjecture
1
and thus it shows that Proposition
5
holds.

Proposition 6
.

Under the Poisson Model
1
, Conjecture
1
holds.

Proof.

The proof is given Appendix
F
.
∎

In Figure
2
we computed the expected number of
{\mathbf{x}}
’s
whose bias multiplied by
\left|\widetilde{H}\right|
is bigger than some prescribed quantity
T
according to

•

the standard independence model in dual attacks where the
\langle\mathbf{e},\mathbf{h}\rangle
’s are supposed to be independent,

•

some experiments,

•

the case were we replace
the right-hand term of
N_{i,j}
(given in Equation (
12
)) by their Poisson model.

As it is shown by Figure
2
, the Poisson model matches remarkably well with the experiments.
This shows, as was the case in the analysis
[
MT23
]
of the
\mathsf{RLPN}
algorithm, that the Poisson model allows to
predict accurately the size of
{\mathscr{S}}
.

Figure 2
.

Size of the set

\{{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\setminus\{{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}{{\mathbf{G}}_{\textup{aux}}}\}\;:\widehat{f_{{\mathbf{y}},{\mathscr{H}}}}\left({\mathbf{x}}\right)\geqslant T\}
as a function of
T

when
[w,{t_{\textup{aux}}},{k_{\textup{aux}}},s,k,n,u,t]=[5,2,20,28,30,60,8,8]
, number of
\mathsf{LPN}
samples
N=65536
.
Here the curve “Independence model” has been replaced when
modelling the
\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle
’s by i.i.d Bernouilli random variables of parameter
\frac{1}{2}
(standard independence model in dual attacks).

7.
Instantiating the Auxiliary Code
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
with an Efficient Decoder

In
\mathsf{double}
-
\mathsf{RLPN}
we need to choose an auxiliary code
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
which is efficiently list-decodable (Definition
6
) at the smallest as possible distance
{t_{\textup{aux}}}=d_{\mathrm{GV}}(s,{k_{\textup{aux}}})
. We propose to use the following product of small random codes (other choices may be more suitable but they are harder to analyze, like polar codes
[
Arı09
,
KU10
,
Şaş11
,
TV12
]
),

{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathcal{C}_{1}\times\cdots\times\mathcal{C}_{b}

where the
\mathcal{C}_{i}
’s are random
\left[\frac{s}{b},\frac{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}{b}\right]
-codes.
Notice that

{\mathcal{D}\mathrm{ec}}({\mathbf{z}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\;:\;\left|{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}+{\mathbf{z}}\right|={t_{\textup{aux}}}\right\}

does not look exactly like how it should with a random code for which our analysis given in Propositions
2
and
5
hold. Furthermore, we will compute

{{\mathscr{H}}}\subseteq\left\{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\times{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\>:\>\forall i\in\left\llbracket 1,b\right\rrbracket,\;|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i)|=\tfrac{w}{b}\mbox{ and }|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i)+{\mathbf{c}}_{i}|=\tfrac{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{b}\right\}

in Instruction
7
of Algorithm
1
. To this aim we will perform exhausting search on the random codes.
By choosing the number
b
of blocs as,

(18)

b=\Theta(\log n)

the above decoding algorithm costs for any parity-check equation
O\left(2^{n/\log n}\right)
(recall that
{t_{\textup{aux}}}\approx d_{\mathrm{GV}}(n,k)
). Therefore, as Algorithm
1
running time is exponential (in
n
) for our considered parameters, it won’t affect it. Furthermore, there are false candidates when computing
{\mathscr{H}}
and it is crucial to estimate their numbers.

Our analysis of Sections
3
and
5
has been made in the idealized-model where
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
is a random code equipped with genie aided decoders. But, by choosing
b
as in Equation (
18
), analysis of Propositions
2
and
5
is still verified with our particular choice of
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
(up to negligible factors) as justified in Appendix
G
.

8.
Links with Dual Attacks in Lattice Based Cryptography

The purpose of this section is to give more details about the close connection between dual attacks in coding theory (
a.k.a

“statistical decoding” after the pioneering work of
[
Jab01
]
) and dual attacks in lattice based cryptography. Basically, with some slight differences
highlighted in
[
PS23
, App. A]
, the lattice based analogue of the dual attack presented here is the slight improvement
[
CST22
]
of the Matzov attack
[
MAT22
]
.
The improvement in
[
CST22
]
is based on the fact that the modulus switching technique used in
[
MAT22
]
can be viewed as a suboptimal source distortion code for the Euclidean metric which can
be replaced by an almost optimal polar code. The approach followed here should carry over to this lattice setting as well and in particular,
the fundamental duality Proposition
4
. Let us just observe now that a simple duality equality (together with a gross approximation based on the considerations of
§
5.2
) can be used to explain the results observed in
[
DP23b
, Fig. 3]
.
It was shown there that predictions of the score function based on standard independence assumptions made for dual attacks in lattice based cryptography seem to be off
in some parameter region (what can be called
the “error-floor” region due to its similarity with the Low-Density-Parity-Check codes literature).
To explain this point, we will use the same notation as in
[
DP23b
]
and will not redefine the quantities appearing here.

Let us first observe that an immediate corollary of Proposition
4
is

Corollary 1
.

Consider an
[n,k]
linear code
\mathcal{C}
and consider some word
{\mathbf{y}}={\mathbf{c}}+{\mathbf{e}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
where
{\mathbf{c}}
is in
\mathcal{C}
. Let

{\mathscr{W}}
be the set of codewords of weight
w
in
\mathcal{C}^{\perp}
and let
f_{\mathscr{W}}({\mathbf{y}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{{\mathbf{w}}\in{\mathscr{W}}}(-1)^{\left\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{w}}\right\rangle}
.
We have

f_{\mathscr{W}}({\mathbf{y}})=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\sum_{i=0}^{n}N_{i}K_{w}^{n}(i)

where
N_{i}
is the number of words of weight
i
in
\mathcal{C}+{\mathbf{e}}
.

It is insightful to view these Krawtchouk polynomials as the
Fourier transform of the indicator function of a Hamming sphere, see Fact
1
. Similarly, the lattice based analogue of Corollary
1

involving the lattice based analogue of
f_{\mathscr{W}}
, which is called the score function in

[
DP23b
]
will involve the Bessel function of the first kind (see for instance
[
DDRT23
, Fact 4.9]
). We namely obtain

Proposition 7
.

Consider a lattice
\Lambda\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
and consider some word
\mathbf{y}=\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
where
\mathbf{x}
is in
\Lambda
. Let
\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}
be the set of dual lattice vectors of Euclidean weights in
(w-\varepsilon,w+\varepsilon)
in
\Lambda^{\vee}
and let

f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}({\mathbf{y}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{\mathbf{w}\in\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}\cos(2\pi\langle\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\rangle)
. We have

(19)

\displaystyle f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}(\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda^{\vee}|}\;\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\frac{N_{j}}{j^{n}\;(2\pi)^{n/2}}\;\Big((2\pi(w+\varepsilon)j)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(2\pi(w+\varepsilon)j)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad-(2\pi(w-\varepsilon)j)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(2\pi(w-\varepsilon)j)\Big)

where
N_{j}
is the number of words of Euclidean norm
j
in
\Lambda+\mathbf{e}
and
J_{\nu}
is the Bessel function of the first kind of order
1
1

1

Here the
j
’s belong to the discrete set of all possible norms in the lattice and should not be viewed as an integer value.

\nu
.

The proof is given in Appendix
H
.
Let us take some subset
{\mathscr{W}}
of
\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}
of size
N
say. We make the approximation
f_{{{{\mathscr{W}}}}}({\mathbf{y}})\approx\frac{N}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}\right|}\;f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}({\mathbf{y}})
and by using the
Gaussian heuristic and some computations that are detailed in Appendix
H
:

(20)

f_{{\mathscr{W}}}(\mathbf{y})\approx N\frac{\sqrt{n\pi}}{e}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}N_{j}\left(\frac{n}{2\pi ewj}\right)^{n/2-1}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj).

We can use now a similar heuristic as the one described in §
5.2
and predict that the abnormal large values of
the score function
f_{{\mathscr{W}}}(\mathbf{y})
appear when
{\mathbf{y}}
is abnormally close to
\Lambda
, say
N_{\leqslant x}\neq 0
, where

N_{\leqslant x}=\left|\{{\mathbf{c}}\in\Lambda:\left|{\mathbf{y}}-{\mathbf{c}}\right|\leqslant x\}\right|
when
\mathbb{P}\left(N_{\leqslant x}\neq 0\right)\ll 1
.
In this case, we make the crude approximation that the sum (
20
) is dominated by the term
j_{0}
which is the smallest in it:

(21)

f_{{\mathscr{W}}}(\mathbf{y})\approx N\frac{\sqrt{n\pi}}{e}N_{j_{0}}\left(\frac{n}{2\pi ewj_{0}}\right)^{n/2-1}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj_{0}).

The survival function
\mathbb{P}\left(f_{{\mathscr{W}}}(\mathbf{y})\geqslant X\right)
is then crudely approximated as the probability that such an event happens

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(f_{{\mathscr{W}}}(\mathbf{y})\geqslant N\frac{\sqrt{n\pi}}{e}N_{x}\left(\frac{n}{2\pi ewx}\right)^{n/2-1}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wx)\right)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(N_{\leqslant x}\geqslant 1\right)

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(N_{\leqslant x})

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\left(\frac{x}{\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{2\pi e}\cdot(\pi n)^{1/n}}\cdot|\Lambda|^{1/n}}\right)^{n}

where the last approximation is the Gaussian heuristic.
In the context of the experiments described in
[
DP23b
, §5]
,
\Lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathcal{L}\left(\boldsymbol{\rm B}\right)
is given by
2
2

2

In
[
DP23b
]
,
\Lambda
and
\boldsymbol{\rm B}
are actually respectively
\Lambda^{\prime}
and
\boldsymbol{\rm B}^{\prime}

(22)

\boldsymbol{\rm B}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}{\mathbf{I}}_{n/2}&\boldsymbol{\rm A}\\
\hline\cr\boldsymbol{\rm 0}&q\cdot{\mathbf{I}}_{n/2}\end{array}\right]\cdot\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}2\cdot{\mathbf{I}}_{k_{\mathrm{fft}}}&\boldsymbol{\rm 0}\\
\hline\cr\boldsymbol{\rm 0}&{\mathbf{I}}_{n-k_{\mathrm{fft}}}\end{array}\right]

Then we use the same
full sieve
algorithm as in
[
DP23b
]
to produce short vectors
{{{\mathscr{W}}}}\subset\Lambda^{\vee}
.
In what follows, we use the practical values of
N
and
w
that we obtained by experiments. We have reused the implementation for the experiments in
[
DP23b
, §5]
3
3

3

https://github.com/ludopulles/DoesDualSieveWork
.
This very crude estimation seems to capture the error-floor behavior of the survival function as shown in Figure
3
. The point is that it is precisely this part of the curve which is not predicted by the standard independence assumption and which had no explanation so far. It can also be observed that the duality result is nothing but a straighforward use of the Poisson formula which has also be
used very recently in
[
WE23
]
to predict the abnormal variance of the BDD score distribution observed in
[
DP23b
, Table 1]
.

Figure 3
.
Crude estimation of the survival function (red, dash-dot line) compared to the experiments in
[
DP23b
, §5]
(green, full line) and the prediction with the standard independence assumption (blue, dashed line). (left)
q=3329
,
n=60
,
T=2^{45}
,
N=5040
and
w=0.0320
; (right)
q=3329
,
n=80
,
T=2^{48}
,
N=89494
and
w=0.0376
.

A more precise prediction.
One can remark (see Figure
3
) that
i)
our newly introduced approximate distribution for
f_{{\mathscr{W}}}
given in Equation (
21
) matches the experimental curves specifically in the waterfall-floor zone and
ii)
the distribution of
f_{{\mathscr{W}}}
given by the independence heuristic
[
DP23b
, Heuristic 3]
matches the experimental curves up to this waterfall-floor zone. A natural idea to predict the experimental curve on the whole support is therefore to take the convolution of these two distributions. Indeed, for any support point, there is always one distribution which exponentially dominates the other one.

Distribution in the waterfall-floor zone.

Let us denote by
\mathbf{X}_{\textup{floor}}
the random variable given by Equation (
21
), namely:

(23)

\mathbf{X}_{\textup{floor}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}G(j_{0})

where

G(j)=N\frac{\sqrt{n\pi}}{e}\left(\frac{n}{2\pi ewj}\right)^{n/2-1}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj)

and
j_{0}
is the length of the shortest vector of the lattice.
We only have to compute the distribution of
j_{0}
to compute the distribution of
\mathbf{X}_{\textup{floor}}
. To that extend we make the following classic model.

Model 2
.

Model for the number of lattice points in a ball.
Let
\Lambda
be a random lattice of full rank
n
and of volume
V
. We make the model that:

|\Lambda\setminus\{0\}\cap\mathcal{B}_{z}|\sim\mathrm{Poisson}\left(\frac{\mathrm{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}_{1}\right)z^{n}}{V}\right)

where
\mathcal{B}_{z}
denotes the Euclidean ball of center
\mathbf{0}
and radius
z
and

\mathrm{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}_{1}\right)=\frac{\sqrt{\pi^{n}}}{\Gamma\left(\frac{n}{2}+1\right)}
.

This model allows us to write the following fact regarding the distribution of the length of the
j
’th shortest vector:

Fact 2
.

Under Model
2
, and for
k=0\cdots\infty
the distribution of
j_{k}
, i.e. the
k^{\prime}th
non-zero shortest vector of a random lattice
\Lambda
of full rank
n
and of volume
V
, is given by:

j_{k}^{n}\sim\mathrm{Gamma}\left(k+1,\;\frac{\mathrm{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}_{1}\right)}{V}\right)

where
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathrm{Gamma}\left(b,\theta\right)
has the following survival function when
b
is an integer:

\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{Z}\geqslant\alpha\right)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}e^{-\theta\alpha}\sum_{i=0}^{b-1}\frac{\left(\theta\alpha\right)^{i}}{i!}.

Proof.

For any
z>0
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(j_{k}^{n}>z^{n}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(j_{k}>z\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{i=0}^{k}``|\Lambda\setminus\{0\}\cap\mathcal{B}_{z}|=i"\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\mathbb{P}\left(|\Lambda\setminus\{0\}\cap\mathcal{B}_{z}|=i\right)

(disjoint union)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{k}\frac{(\mathrm{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}_{1}\right)\;z^{n}/V)^{i}\>e^{-(\mathrm{Vol}\left(\mathcal{B}_{1}\right)\;z^{n}/V)}}{i\>!}

(Model
2
)

which completes the proof.
∎

Distribution in the waterfall zone.

Let us denote by
\mathbf{X}_{\textup{fall}}
the random variable
f_{{\mathscr{W}}}
under the independence heuristic. As given by
[
DP23b
, Lemma 3]
and the discussion that follows their lemma we have

Fact 3
.

\mathbf{X}_{\textup{fall}}
follows a normal distribution of mean
0
and variance
\frac{1}{2}N
. More precisely, its probability density function
p
is given by

p(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\pi N}}e^{-x^{2}/N}.

We now make the refined model that
f_{{\mathscr{W}}}\left({\mathbf{y}}\right)
follows the same distribution as
\mathbf{X}_{\textup{fall}}+\mathbf{X}_{\textup{floor}}
where the distribution of
\mathbf{X}_{\textup{floor}}
is computed numerically by using Fact
2
along with Equation (
23
). We show in Figure
4
that the distribution of this refined model well approximates the behavior of the experimental distribution of
f_{{\mathscr{W}}}
on the whole support.

.

[
DP23b
, §5]
(green, full line) and the prediction with the standard independence assumption (blue, dashed line). (left)

,
n=60
,
T=2^{45}
,
N=5040
and
w=0.0320
; (right)
q=3329
,
n=80
,
T=2^{48}
,
N=89494
and
w=0.0376

Concurrent work.

Note that very recently we have been made aware of the concurrent work
[
DP23a
]
which similarly to what we do here, uses Bessel functions to predict the score with a related approach
and similar predictions (see
[
DP23a
, §4.3]
).

Appendix A
Proof of Proposition
2

Let us first recall this proposition
See
2

The proof of this Proposition
2
will be a consequence of the following lemma.

Lemma 2
.

Let
w,s,k,{k_{\textup{aux}}},t_{\textup{aux}},n\in\mathbb{N}
be such that (for some constant
a>0
)

(24)

\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{k}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)

Let
{\mathscr{N}}
be a fixed set of
n-s
positions in
\left\llbracket 1,n\right\rrbracket
and
{\mathscr{P}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket\backslash{\mathscr{N}}
. Let
\mathbf{e}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}
be some error of weight
u
on
{\mathscr{N}}
.

Assume that
\mathcal{C}
is an
[n,k]
-linear code chosen by picking an
(n-k)\times n
binary parity-check matrix
{\mathbf{H}}
uniformly at random and that
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
is chosen by picking an
(s-{k_{\textup{aux}}})\times s
binary parity-check matrix
{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}
uniformly at random.
Let us define for
b\in\{0,1\}
,

\displaystyle E_{b}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left|\left\{({\mathbf{e}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{h}})\in\mathcal{S}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{s}\times\mathcal{C}^{\perp}:\;\;\left|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\right|=w\;\mbox{ and }\;{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\;\langle{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{N}}},\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle=b\right\}\right|,

\displaystyle E^{\prime}_{b}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left|\left\{\left(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}\right)\in\mathcal{S}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{s}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}:\;|\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{N}}}|=w\;\mbox{ and }\;\;\langle\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{N}}},\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle=b\right\}\right|.

Then,

(25)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}(E_{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Theta\left(1\right)\frac{E^{\prime}_{b}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

(26)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}(E_{b})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)\frac{E^{\prime}_{b}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Proof.

Let
\mathbf{1}_{{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{h}}}
be the indicator function of the event “
{\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}
and
{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
”. Define

\mathcal{E}_{b}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\left(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}\right)\in\mathcal{S}_{t_{\textup{aux}}}^{s}\times\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}\mbox{: }|\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{N}}}|=w\mbox{ and }\langle{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{N}}},\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle=b\right\}.

Notice that
E_{b}^{\prime}=\left|\mathcal{E}_{b}\right|
.
By definition and linearity of the expectation

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(E_{b}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\sum_{(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{h}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}(\mathbf{1}_{{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{h}}})

(27)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

We have that,

(28)

\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{cl}\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}&\;\;\mbox{if }\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{0}\\
\frac{1}{2^{k}}&\;\;\mbox{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

Indeed, notice that events “
{\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}
” and “
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
” are independent. Furthermore,
\mathbf{h}
cannot be equal to
\mathbf{0}
as
|\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{N}}}|=w>0
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H}}\left({\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}\right)\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

Now, plugging Equation (
28
) in (
27
) leads to,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(E_{b}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}\\
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\neq\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}\\
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}=\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{k}}

(29)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{s}}\left(\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\right|}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\right|}{2^{k}}\right)

where for a fixed
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
,

(30)

{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}:\;(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\neq\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\right\},

(31)

{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}:\;(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}=\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\right\}.

Notice that for any
\mathbf{e}^{\prime}
,

(32)

\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}\right|=\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}\right|}{2^{s}}

It is readily seen that,

\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\right|=\sum_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{s}}\left(\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\right|+\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\right|\right)

which implies that

\sum_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{s}}\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\right|=\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\right|}{1+\frac{1}{2^{s}}}=\Theta\left(1\right)\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\right|\quad\mbox{and}\quad\sum_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{s}}\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\right|=\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\right|}{2^{s}-1}=\Theta\left(2^{-s}\right)\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\right|

Plugging this into Equation (
29
) leads to,

(33)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(E_{b}\right)=\Theta\left(1\right)\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\right|}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+\Theta\left(1\right)\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\right|}{2^{k+s}}=\Theta\left(1\right)\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\right|}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

which shows Equation (
25
). Let us show now Equation (
26
). By definition

\displaystyle\mathbf{Var}\left(E_{b}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\mathbf{Var}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}}\right)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}),(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{E_{b}^{\prime}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}),(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)

where we used that
\mathbf{Var}(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}})\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{h}}^{2}\right)=\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e},\mathbf{h}}\right)
and Equation (
33
).

To compute the above expectations, we will split in two cases according to
{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}
and
{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}
which are respectively defined in Equations (
30
) and (
31
). More precisely, we will fix
\mathbf{e}_{b}^{\prime}
and suppose that
\mathbf{h}^{b}
belongs to
{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}
or
{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}
. We will treat the following disjoint cases.

1.

{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}=\left\{(\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}):\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}
,

2.

{\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}=\left\{(\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}):\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}

3.

{\mathscr{F}}_{2}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}=\left\{(\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}):\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}

4.

{\mathscr{F}}_{3}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}=\left\{(\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}):\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}

In particular,

(34)

\mathbf{Var}\left(E_{b}\right)\leqslant\frac{E_{b}^{\prime}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\Big(\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}F_{0}}\\
+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}F_{1}}\\
+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{2}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}F_{2}}\\
+\underbrace{\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{3}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}F_{3}}\Big)

Let,

(35)

\textup{Cov}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}F_{0}+F_{1}+F_{2}+F_{3}

For each cases, we will split according to the following subcases.

i.

\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
,

ii.

\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
,

iii.

\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}

iv.

\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}

v.

\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}

vi.

\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}

Case
1
:
Recall that in this case we have

(36)

\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\neq\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}

We have,

\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

Let us compute
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)
when
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})
. By definition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot},\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}F_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

(37)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\textup{Cov}^{(0)}

where,

\textup{Cov}^{(0)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}},\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

Our aim now it to upper-bound
\textup{Cov}^{(0)}
according to the above
6
sub-cases.

Sub-case
i
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}\qquad(\mbox{as }\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

where in the last line we used Equation (
36
).
Therefore, in that case

\textup{Cov}^{(0)}\leqslant\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

Sub-case
ii
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

(38)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

where in the last line we used that
\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}
showing that these two vectors are linearly independent (we work in
\mathbb{F}_{2}
), and thus that both events are independent. But, as they are different from
\mathbf{0}
(according to Equation (
36
)) we have for (
b\in\{0,1\}
)

\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{b}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{b}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)=\frac{1}{2^{s-k_{\textup{aux}}}}

Therefore, plugging this in Equation (
38
) leads to

\textup{Cov}^{(0)}=0.

Sub-case
iii
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}
. In that case,

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\left(\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

where in the second equality we used that
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
and are different from
\mathbf{0}
(according to Equation (
36
)) which implies that both events are independent.
Therefore, in that case

(39)

\textup{Cov}^{(0)}\leqslant\frac{1}{2^{k+2s-2{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Sub-case
iv
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. In that case,

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=0

where in the second equality we used that
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
which implies that both events are independent.

Sub-case
v
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
,
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
and
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}={\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}
. We have

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

Therefore,

\textup{Cov}^{(0)}\leqslant\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Sub-case
vi
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
,
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
and
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}
.
In that case we can write

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}-\left(\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)^{2}

Therefore we obtain,

\textup{Cov}^{(0)}=0.

Case
2
:
Recall that in this case we have

(40)

\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}=\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}

We have,

\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}=\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Let us compute
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)
when
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})
. By definition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot},\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}F_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

(41)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\textup{Cov}^{(1)}

where,

\textup{Cov}^{(1)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}},\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Our aim now it to upper-bound
\textup{Cov}^{(1)}
according to the above
6
sub-cases.

Sub-case
i
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. This subcase is impossible according to Equation (
40
). Therefore,

\textup{Cov}^{(1)}=0.

Sub-case
ii
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

(42)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

where in the last line we used that
\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}
showing that these two vectors are linearly independent (we work in
\mathbb{F}_{2}
), and thus that both events are independent. Furthermore, we also used that
\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{0}
according to Equation (
40
). But,

\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)=\frac{1}{2^{s-k_{\textup{aux}}}}

Therefore, plugging this in Equation (
42
) leads to

\textup{Cov}^{(1)}=0.

Sub-case
iii
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}
. In that case,

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0}\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k}}\;\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

where in the second equality we used that
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
which implies that both events are independent. Furthermore, we also used that
\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{0}
according to Equation (
40
).
Therefore, in that case

(43)

\textup{Cov}^{(1)}\leqslant\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Sub-case
iv
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. In that case,

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=0

where in the second equality we used that
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
which implies that both events are independent. Furthermore, we also used that
\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{0}
according to Equation (
40
).

Sub-case
v
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
,
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
and
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}={\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}
. This subcase is impossible according to Equation (
40
). Therefore,

\textup{Cov}^{(1)}=0

Sub-case
vi
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
,
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
and
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}
.
In that case we can write

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(0)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}-\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Therefore we obtain,

\textup{Cov}^{(1)}=0.

Case
3
:
This situation is symmetric to Case
2
.

Case
4
:
Recall that in this case we have

(44)

\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}=\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}

We have,

\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\right)\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

Let us compute
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)
when
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})
. By definition

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot},\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}F_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{3}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}\left(\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}}\;\mathbf{1}_{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

(45)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{3}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\\
(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\neq(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\end{subarray}}\textup{Cov}^{(3)}

where,

\textup{Cov}^{(3)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H}}\left({\mathbf{h}}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}\in\mathcal{C}^{\bot}\right)\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}},\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

Our aim now it to upper-bound
\textup{Cov}^{(1)}
according to the above
6
sub-cases.

Sub-case
i
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. We have

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{0}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}-\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

where in the second equality we used Equation (
44
).
Therefore, in that case

\textup{Cov}^{(3)}=0.

Sub-case
ii
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}=\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. According to Equation (
44
) this sub-case is impossible. Therefore,

\textup{Cov}^{(3)}=0.

Sub-case
iii
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}
. According to Equation (
44
) this sub-case is impossible. Therefore,

\textup{Cov}^{(3)}=0.

Sub-case
iv
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
and
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}=\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
. According to Equation (
44
) this sub-case is impossible. Therefore,

\textup{Cov}^{(3)}=0.

Sub-case
v
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
,
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
and
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}={\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}
. We have

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\;\mathbb{P}_{{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{0}\in{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)-\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{2k}}-\frac{1}{2^{2k}}

where in the second equality we used Equation (
44
).
Therefore,

\textup{Cov}^{(3)}=0

Sub-case
vi
.
Suppose that
\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}
,
\mathbf{h}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}
,
\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}\neq\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}
and
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{0}+{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{1}+{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}
. According to Equation (
44
) this sub-case is impossible. Therefore,

\textup{Cov}^{(3)}=0.

We are now ready to gather Cases
1
,
2
,
3
and
4
according to Subcases
i
,
ii
,
iii
,
iv
,
v
and
vi
. Our aim is to bound

Cov

that were defined in Equation
35
.
We can already notice that Case
4
has no impact on this sum while Cases
2
and
3
have an influence only in Subcase
iii
. Furthermore,
ii
,
iv
and
vi
have no contribution to this sum, whatever is the considered case.

Let us upper-bound
Cov
according to the different subcases where
\textup{Cov}_{i}
denotes the terms involved in
Cov
coming from Subcase
i
(in particular
\textup{Cov}_{i}
is defined as a certain sum of
\textup{Cov}^{(i)}
, see Equations (
37
), (
41
) and (
38
)).

Subcase
i
:
We have,

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}_{1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{1}:(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}\\
\mathbf{h}^{1}\neq\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}=\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{2k}}\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{(\mathbf{e}_{0},\mathbf{h}^{0})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}}{2^{k}}\frac{E_{b}^{\prime}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Subcase
ii
:
We have,

\textup{Cov}_{2}=0.

Subcase
iii
:
We have here to split the computation here between Cases
1
and
2
. Recall that they are given by

{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}=\left\{(\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}):\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\right\},

{\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}=\left\{(\mathbf{h}^{0},\mathbf{h}^{1}):\;\mathbf{h}^{0}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\right\}

where,

{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}:\;(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\neq\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\right\},

{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}^{\mathbf{e}^{\prime}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\mathbf{h}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}:\;(\mathbf{e}^{\prime},\mathbf{h})\in{\mathscr{E}}_{b}\mbox{ and }\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{P}}}=\mathbf{e}^{\prime}\right\}.

But recall according to Equation (
32
) that,

\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,1}\right|=\frac{\left|{\mathscr{E}}_{b,2}\right|}{2^{s}}

Therefore, in Subcase
iii
,

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}_{3}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\textup{Cov}^{(0)}+2\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\textup{Cov}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{k+2s-2{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+2\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{1}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\quad\mbox{(Equations \eqref{eq:subcase03}) and \eqref{eq:subcase13}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{k+2s-2{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+\frac{1}{2^{s}}\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)\sum_{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime}\neq\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{h}^{0}=\mathbf{h}^{1}\in{\mathscr{F}}_{0}^{\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{k+2s-2{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

where we basically use the same reasoning than for proving Equation (
33
). There in this subcase,

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}_{3}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}\\
\mathbf{h}^{1}=\mathbf{h}^{0},{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{k+2s-2{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\frac{\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{k+2s-2{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\;\frac{E_{b}^{\prime}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Subcase
iv
:
We have,

\textup{Cov}_{4}=0.

Subcase
v
:
We have,

\displaystyle\textup{Cov}_{5}

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{(\mathbf{e}_{0}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{0})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{e}_{1}^{\prime},\mathbf{h}^{1})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}\\
\mathbf{h}^{1}\neq\mathbf{h}^{0},\;\mathbf{h}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\neq\mathbf{h}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}},\;{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}\neq{\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}\\
{\mathbf{e}}_{0}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{h}}^{0}_{{\mathscr{P}}}={\mathbf{e}}_{1}^{\prime}+{\mathbf{h}}^{1}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\leqslant\sum_{(\mathbf{e}_{0},\mathbf{h}^{0})\in\mathcal{E}_{b}}\frac{\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}\binom{n-s}{w}}{2^{2k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{k}}\;\frac{E_{b}^{\prime}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

Subcase
vi
:
We have,

\textup{Cov}_{6}=0.

Plugging all these bounds on the
\textup{Cov}_{i}
together and using that
\textup{Cov}=\sum_{i}\textup{Cov}_{i}
leads to

Cov

\displaystyle\leqslant\left(\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}}{2^{k}}+\frac{\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{2^{k}}\right)\;\frac{E_{b}^{\prime}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)\frac{E_{b}^{\prime}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

where in the last lines we used the constraints (
24
) given in the proposition. Plugging this equation in Equation (
34
) concludes the proof.
∎

We are now ready to prove our proposition:

Proof of Proposition
2
.

Let
E_{b}
and
E_{b}^{\prime}
(for
b\in\{0,1\}
) be defined as in Lemma
2
.
By using the Bienaymé-Tchebychev inequality, we obtain for any function
f
mapping the positive integers to positive real numbers:

\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{H},{{\mathbf{H}}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(|E_{b}-\mathbb{E}\left(E_{b}\right)|\geqslant\sqrt{f(n)\mathbb{E}\left(E_{b}\right)}\right)\leqslant\frac{\mathbf{Var}(E_{b})}{f(n)\mathbb{E}(E_{b})}=\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)}{f(n)}

where the last inequality is a consequence of Lemma
2
. Since,

\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle\right)=\frac{E_{0}-E_{1}}{E_{0}+E_{1}},

we have with probability greater than

1-\frac{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)}{f(n)}
that

(46)

\frac{\mu_{0}-\mu_{1}-\sqrt{2f(n)}\sqrt{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}}}{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}+\sqrt{2f(n)}\sqrt{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}}}\leqslant\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle\right)\\
\leqslant\frac{\mu_{0}-\mu_{1}+\sqrt{2f(n)}\sqrt{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}}}{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}-\sqrt{2f(n)}\sqrt{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}}}

where

\mu_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}\left(E_{i}\right)

and where we used that for all positive
x
and
y
,
\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}\leqslant\sqrt{2(x+y)}
.
Let,

N=\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}

It is readily seen that,

N=\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

We let

f(n)=\delta\sqrt{N}/2
. Since
N=\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}
this implies
f(n)=\delta\sqrt{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}}/2
. By Equation (
7
), note that
\frac{f(n)}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)}
tends to infinity as

n
tends to infinity. We notice that

\displaystyle\sqrt{2f(n)}\sqrt{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}}

\displaystyle=\delta^{1/2}(\mu_{0}+\mu_{1})^{3/4}

\displaystyle=o\left(\delta(\mu_{0}+\mu_{1})\right)

because

\frac{\delta^{1/2}(\mu_{0}+\mu_{1})^{3/4}}{\delta(\mu_{0}+\mu_{1})}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\delta\sqrt{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}}}}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2f(n)}}\mathop{\longrightarrow}\limits_{n\to+\infty}0.

Equation (
46
) can now be rewritten as

(47)

\frac{\mu_{0}-\mu_{1}-o\left(\delta(\mu_{0}+\mu_{1})\right)}{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}+o\left(\delta(\mu_{0}+\mu_{1})\right)}\leqslant\bias\left(\langle{\mathbf{e}}^{\prime\prime},\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{N}}},\mathbf{h}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle\right)\\
\leqslant\frac{\mu_{0}-\mu_{1}+o\left(\delta(\mu_{0}+\mu_{1})\right)}{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}-o\left(\delta(\mu_{0}+\mu_{1})\right)}

Now on the other hand

\displaystyle\delta

\displaystyle=\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{E^{\prime}_{0}-E^{\prime}_{1}}{E^{\prime}_{0}+E^{\prime}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\frac{E^{\prime}_{0}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}-\frac{E^{\prime}_{1}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}}{\frac{E^{\prime}_{0}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}+\frac{E^{\prime}_{1}}{2^{k+s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mu_{0}-\mu_{1}}{\mu_{0}+\mu_{1}}

where the last equality is a consequence of Lemma
2
, in particular Equation (
25
).
From this it follows that we can rewrite (
47
) as

\frac{\delta}{1+o(\delta)}-o(\delta)\leqslant\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle\right)\\
\leqslant\frac{\delta}{1-o(\delta)}+o(\delta)
