from which it follows immediately that

\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle\right)=\delta(1+o(1))

which concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Correctness and Running-Time of the
\mathsf{double}
-
\mathsf{RLPN}
algorithm (Algorithm
4
)

In this section we prove the correctness of Algorithm
4
in Subsection
B.1
. Furthermore, we give its running-time in Subsection
B.2
. To this aim, we instantiate Instructions
3
(
ParityCheckEquations
)
7
(
Decode
) of Algorithm
1
.

Notation 3
.

•

Instantiation of Algorithm
4
.

–

We instantiate
ParityCheckEquations
instruction with the technique devised in
[
CDMT22
, §5]
to compute all the parity-checks of a given weight in a code (which is inspired from
[
BJMM12
]
). Its asymptotic complexity is recalled in Proposition
11
.

–

The family of auxiliary
[s,{k_{\textup{aux}}}]
linear codes
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
used will be product of
\log s
random codes as devised in Section
7
. Using these the
Decode
procedure outputs almost all codeword at distance
{t_{\textup{aux}}}
in time
2^{o(s)}\max\left(\frac{\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}},1\right)
.

•

Framework for the analysis of Algorithm
4

–

We prove the correctness (Proposition
8
) and we make the complexity analysis (Proposition
9
) in the framework of Proposition
5
. More specifically, analysis is made for

\mathcal{C}
and
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
being random
[n,k]
and
[s,k{]_{\textup{aux}}}
codes. We argue in Section
7
that the proof would be roughly the same (but more complicated) if we were to make it using
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
being random product codes. The complexity of Algorithm
4
would only grow by a factor of
2^{o(s)}
when using these codes.

–

Note that with the
ParityCheckEquations
and
Decode
procedures we have chosen, Algorithm
4
computes in fact all the possible LPN samples, namely we have (as required in Proposition
5
):

{\mathscr{H}}=\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}

–

We reuse notation introduced in Proposition
5
: the set
{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}
of candidates for the
j
’th auxiliary code
{\mathcal{C}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}
is defined by

(48)

{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\{{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\textup{aux}}}\>:\widehat{f_{{\mathbf{y}},\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}},{{\mathbf{G}}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}}}\left({\mathbf{s}}\right)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\>\widetilde{H}\},

where

(49)

\widetilde{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}},\qquad\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

B.1.
Correctness of the algorithm

The goal of this section is to prove that
\mathsf{double}
-
\mathsf{RLPN}
, namely Algorithm
4
, outputs the desired error vector
{\mathbf{e}}
after essentially
{N}_{\textup{iter}}\approx\frac{\binom{n}{t}}{\binom{s}{t-u}\binom{n-s}{u}}
iterations of the outer loop (Line (
2
) of Algorithm
4
). This is given by the following proposition.

Proposition 8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a code taken uniformly at random among the
[n,k]
linear codes and
{\mathcal{C}^{(1)}_{\textup{aux}}},\dots,{\mathcal{C}^{(N_{\textup{aux}})}_{\textup{aux}}}
which are
N_{\textup{aux}}
codes taken uniformly at random among the
[s,{k_{\textup{aux}}}]
linear codes. Let
{\mathbf{y}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}{\mathbf{c}}+{\mathbf{e}}
where
{\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{C}
and where
{\mathbf{e}}\in\mathcal{S}_{t}^{n}
is a fixed error vector of weight
t
.
As long as the parameters
s,{k_{\textup{aux}}},{t_{\textup{aux}}},w,u
verify the Parameters constraint (
1
) and as long as
N_{\textup{iter}}=\omega\left(\frac{\binom{n}{t}}{\binom{s}{t-u}\binom{n-s}{u}}\right)
and
N_{\textup{aux}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)
, Algorithm
4
outputs the error vector
{\mathbf{e}}
with probability
1-o(1)
.

It is readily seen that when
{N}_{\textup{iter}}=\omega\left(\frac{\binom{n}{t}}{\binom{s}{t-u}\binom{n-s}{u}}\right)
then, with probability
1-o(1)
over the choice of
{\mathscr{P}}
there exists an iteration such that
|{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}|=u
. We only have left to show that for such an iteration we have with high probability that
{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\left({{\mathbf{G}}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)^{\top}\in{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}
for
j=1,\dots,{N}_{\textup{aux}}
which is the purpose of the following lemma.

Lemma 3
.

Let us reuse the setting of Proposition
8
. Moreover, let us fix
{\mathscr{P}}
and
{\mathscr{N}}
two complementary sets of
\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
of size
s
and
n-s
respectively and such that
|{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}|=u
. Let us denote by
{{\mathbf{G}}^{(1)}_{\textup{aux}}},\dots,{{\mathbf{G}}^{(N_{\textup{aux}})}_{\textup{aux}}}
the generators matrices of the codes
{\mathcal{C}^{(1)}_{\textup{aux}}},\dots,{\mathcal{C}^{(N_{\textup{aux}})}_{\textup{aux}}}
respectively. Then,

(50)

\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{j=1}^{N_{\textup{aux}}}``{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\>{{\mathbf{G}}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}^{\intercal}\in{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}"\right)=1-o(1).

Proof.

First, notice that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcap_{j=1}^{{N}_{\textup{aux}}}``{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\>{{\mathbf{G}}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}^{\intercal}\in{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}"\right)

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{j=1}^{{N}_{\textup{aux}}}``{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\>{{\mathbf{G}}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}^{\intercal}\notin{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}"\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant 1-\sum_{j=1}^{{N}_{\textup{aux}}}\mathbb{P}\left(``{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\>{{\mathbf{G}}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}^{\intercal}\notin{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}"\right)

where we used the union-bound.
Now, as
{N}_{\textup{aux}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)
we only have to show that
\mathbb{P}\left(``{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\>{{\mathbf{G}}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}^{\intercal}\notin{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}"\right)=o(1)
to prove Equation (
50
). By using Fact
4
,

"{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\>{{\mathbf{G}}^{(j)}_{\textup{aux}}}^{\intercal}\notin{\mathscr{S}}^{(j)}"\Longleftrightarrow\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}

Our aim is to show,

\mathbb{P}\left(\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)=o(1).

To simplify notation let
b\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)
.
It is readily seen that
\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|\right)=\widetilde{H}\left(1+o(1)\right)
and
\mathbf{Var}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|\right)\leqslant\widetilde{H}(1+o(1))
. Therefore, by using Bienaymé-Tchebychev inequality, for any
a
,

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|-(1+o(1))\widetilde{H}\right|\geqslant\sqrt{a\widetilde{H}}\right)\leqslant\frac{1+o(1)}{a}

We have the following computation,

\mathbb{P}\left(b<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left(b<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\mid\left|\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|-(1+o(1))\widetilde{H}\right|\geqslant\sqrt{a\widetilde{H}}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|-(1+o(1))\widetilde{H}\right|\geqslant\sqrt{a\widetilde{H}}\right)\\
+\mathbb{P}\left(b<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\mid\left|\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|-(1+o(1))\widetilde{H}\right|<\sqrt{a\widetilde{H}}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|-(1+o(1))\widetilde{H}\right|<\sqrt{a\widetilde{H}}\right)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(b<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\left|\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|-(1+o(1))\widetilde{H}\right|\geqslant\sqrt{a\widetilde{H}}\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(b<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{(1+o(1))\widetilde{H}-\sqrt{a\widetilde{H}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{1+o(1)}{a}+\mathbb{P}\left(b<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{1}{(1+o(1))-\sqrt{\frac{a}{\widetilde{H}}}}\right)

Let us choose
a=\widetilde{H}^{1/2}
. Recall that
\widetilde{H}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\omega\mathopen{}\left(\frac{n^{\alpha+8}}{\delta^{2}}\right)
where
\delta\leqslant 1
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(b<\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)

\displaystyle=o(1)+\mathbb{P}\left(b<\frac{\delta}{2}(1+o(1))\right)

\displaystyle=o(1)

where in the last equality we used Proposition
7
. It concludes the proof.
∎

B.2.
Asymptotic complexity of
\mathsf{double}
-
\mathsf{RLPN}

We now have every tool to give the complexity of our algorithm, namely, we can compute the expected number of candidates at each iteration given by Proposition
5
and we have the correctness of our algorithm which is given by Proposition
8
.

Proposition 9
.

Asymptotic complexity exponent of the
\mathsf{double}
-
\mathsf{RLPN}
algorithm. Define

\displaystyle R\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{k}{n},\quad\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{t}{n},\quad\sigma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{s}{n},\quad R_{\textup{aux}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}{n}

\displaystyle\quad\tau_{\textup{aux}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{n},\omega\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{w}{n},\quad\mu\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{u}{n}

Suppose that de
Decode
procedure has an expected time complexity of
2^{n\cdot\sigma\cdot o(1)}
.
The expected complexity of the
\mathsf{double}
-
\mathsf{RLPN}
algorithm to decode a code of rate
R
at relative distance
\tau
is upper bounded by

2^{n\>\left(\alpha_{\textup{$\mathsf{double}$-$\mathsf{RLPN}$\ }}+o(1)\right)}

where

\displaystyle\alpha_{\textup{$\mathsf{double}$-$\mathsf{RLPN}$\ }}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle-\pi+\max\left(\left(1-\sigma\right)\>\cdot\>\alpha_{\textup{eq}}\left(\frac{R-\sigma}{1-\sigma},\frac{\omega}{1-\sigma}\right),\;\nu_{\textup{sample}},\;R_{\textup{aux}},\;\nu_{\textup{candidate}}\>\cdot\>N_{\textup{aux}}+\alpha_{\textup{ISD}}\right)

where

\displaystyle\pi

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle h\left(\tau\right)-\sigma\>\cdot\>h_{2}\left(\frac{\tau-\mu}{\sigma}\right)-(1-\sigma)\>\cdot\>h\left(\frac{\mu}{1-\sigma}\right),

\displaystyle\nu_{\textup{samples}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\left(1-\sigma\right)\>\cdot\>h_{2}\left(\frac{\omega}{1-\sigma}\right)+\sigma\;\cdot\;h_{2}\left(\frac{\tau_{\textup{aux}}}{\sigma}\right)-(R-R_{\textup{aux}}),

\displaystyle\alpha_{\textup{ISD}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\max\left(\sigma\>\cdot\>\alpha_{\textup{ISD-Dumer}}\left(1-\frac{N_{\textup{aux}}\cdot\tau_{\textup{aux}}}{\sigma},\frac{\tau-\mu}{\sigma}\right),\nu_{\textup{ISD}}+\left(1-\sigma\right)\;\cdot\;\alpha_{\textup{ISD-Dumer}}\left(\frac{R-\sigma}{1-\sigma},\frac{\mu}{1-\sigma}\right)\right),

\displaystyle\nu_{\textup{ISD}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\max\left(\sigma\>\cdot\>h_{2}\left(\frac{\tau-\mu}{\sigma}\right)-N_{\textup{aux}}\>\cdot\>\tau_{\textup{aux}},\>0\right)

\displaystyle\nu_{\textup{candidates}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}

\displaystyle\max\left(\max_{(\eta,\zeta)\in\mathcal{A}}\sigma\>\cdot\>h_{2}\left(\frac{\zeta}{\sigma}\right)+(1-\sigma)\>\cdot\>h_{2}\left(\frac{\eta}{1-\sigma}\right)-(1-R),\;0\right),

with

\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\Bigg\{(\eta,\zeta)\in\left[0,1-\sigma\right]\times\left[0,\sigma\right]\>:\sigma\left[\widetilde{\kappa}\left(\frac{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{\sigma},\frac{\tau-\mu}{\sigma}\right)-\widetilde{\kappa}\left(\frac{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{\sigma},\frac{\zeta}{\sigma}\right)\right]+\\
(1-\sigma)\left[\widetilde{\kappa}\left(\frac{\omega}{1-\sigma},\frac{\mu}{1-\sigma}\right)-\widetilde{\kappa}\left(\frac{\omega}{1-\sigma},\frac{\eta}{1-\sigma}\right)\right]\leqslant 0\Bigg\},

and

•

\alpha_{\textup{eq}}(R^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
is the complexity exponent of
ParityCheckEquations
to compute all parity-checks of relative weight
\tau^{\prime}
of a code of rate
R^{\prime}
. It is instantiated here with a technique devised in
[
CDMT22
, §5 Eq. (5.4)]
and its complexity is recalled in Proposition
11
.

•

\alpha_{\textup{ISD-Dumer}}(R^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})
is the complexity exponent of
Decode-Dumer
to return all the solutions to the decoding problem in a code of rate
R^{\prime}
at relative distance
\tau^{\prime}
. Its complexity is recalled in Proposition
10
.

Moreover,
\sigma,\>R_{\textup{aux}},\>\tau_{\textup{aux}},\>\omega,\>\mu,
are non-negative and such that

\sigma\leqslant R,\quad\tau-\sigma\leqslant\mu\leqslant\tau,\quad\omega\leqslant 1-\sigma,

(51)

\displaystyle\nu_{\textup{samples}}

\displaystyle\geqslant-2\>\varepsilon_{\textup{bias}},

(52)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geqslant(1-\sigma)\>h_{2}\left(\frac{\omega}{1-\sigma}\right)+\sigma h_{2}\left(\frac{\tau_{\textup{aux}}}{\sigma}\right)-R

(53)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\geqslant\sigma\>h_{2}\left(\frac{\tau_{\textup{aux}}}{\sigma}\right)-(\sigma-R_{\textup{aux}})

where
\widetilde{\kappa}
is the function defined in Proposition
1
and

\varepsilon_{\textup{bias}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\>\sigma\>\left[\widetilde{\kappa}\left(\frac{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{\sigma},\frac{\tau-\mu}{\sigma}\right)-h_{2}\left(\frac{\tau_{\textup{aux}}}{\sigma}\right)\right]+\>(1-\sigma)\>\left[\widetilde{\kappa}\left(\frac{\omega}{1-\sigma},\frac{\mu}{1-\sigma}\right)-h_{2}\left(\frac{\omega}{1-\sigma}\right)\right].

Finally, we require that
N_{\textup{aux}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)
.

Remark 5
.

In practice our parameters are such that we decode the auxiliary code at Gilbert-Varshamov distance, namely
\tau_{\textup{aux}}=\sigma\>h_{2}^{-1}\left(1-R_{\textup{aux}}\right)
.

While initially Dumer’s decoder
[
Dum91
]
is designed to produce only one solution to the decoding problem it suffices to re-run it as many time as the number of solutions we expect from the decoding problem to find all of them. We get the following proposition giving the asymptotic complexity of the
Decode-Dumer
procedure.

Proposition 10

(Asymptotic time complexity of ISD Decoder
[
Dum91
]
to produce
all
solutions to the decoding problem)
.

Let

R\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{k}{n},\tau\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{t}{n}
. Let
\ell
and
w
be two (implicit) parameters of the algorithm and define
\lambda\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{\ell}{n},\omega\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\lim\limits_{n\to\infty}\frac{w}{n}
.
The time and space complexities of
[
Dum91
]
to find a proportion
1-o(1)
of all solutions to the decoding problem at distance
t
on an
[n,k]
linear code are given by
2^{n\>\left(\alpha_{\textup{ISD-Dumer}}+o(1)\right)}
and
2^{n\>\left(\beta_{\textup{ISD-Dumer}}+o(1)\right)}
respectively where

(54)

\displaystyle\alpha_{\textup{ISD-Dumer}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\min_{\omega,\lambda}\left(\pi+\max\left(\frac{R+\lambda}{2}h_{2}\left(\frac{\omega}{R+\lambda}\right),\left(R+\lambda\right)h_{2}\left(\frac{\omega}{R+\lambda}\right)-\lambda\right)\right),

(55)

\displaystyle\pi

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}h_{2}(\tau)-(1-R-\lambda)h_{2}\left(\frac{\tau-\omega}{1-R-\lambda}\right)-(R+\lambda)h_{2}\left(\frac{\omega}{R+\lambda}\right),

(56)

\displaystyle\nu_{\textup{sol}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\max\left(h_{2}\left(\tau\right)-(1-R),0\right),

(57)

\displaystyle\beta_{\textup{ISD-Dumer}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{R+\lambda}{2}h_{2}\left(\frac{\omega}{R+\lambda}\right).

Moreover
\lambda
and
\omega
must verify the following constraints:

0\leqslant\lambda\leqslant 1-R,\qquad\max\left(R+\lambda+\tau-1,0\right)\leqslant\omega\leqslant\min\left(\tau,R+\lambda\right).

The expected number of solutions is given by
2^{n\>\left(\nu_{\textup{sol}}+o(1)\right)}
.

We recall here the asymptotic complexity of the technique devised in
[
CDMT22
, §5, Equation (5.4)]
based on
[
BJMM12
]
decoder to produce all parity-checks of low weight of a code.

Proposition 11
.

Asymptotic time complexity exponent of BJMM technique
[
BJMM12
]
,
[
CDMT22
, §5, Equation (5.4)]
to produce all parity-checks of relative weight
\omega
of a code of rate
R

(58)

\alpha_{\text{BJMM}}(R,\omega)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\min_{\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2}}\gamma

where

\displaystyle\gamma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\max(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3})

\displaystyle\gamma_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\max\left(\nu_{0},\>2\>\nu_{0}-\lambda_{1}\right),\quad\gamma_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\max\left(\nu_{1},\>2\>\nu_{1}-\left(\lambda_{2}-\lambda_{1}\right)\right),\quad\gamma_{3}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\max\left(\nu_{2},\>2\>\nu_{2}-\left(\lambda-\lambda_{2}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\nu_{0}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{h(\pi_{1})}{2},\quad\nu_{1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}h(\pi_{1})-\lambda_{1},\quad\nu_{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}h(\pi_{2})-\lambda_{2},\quad\nu_{3}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}h(\omega)-\lambda.

and the constraint region
{\mathscr{R}}
is defined by the sub-region of nonnegative tuples
(\pi_{1},\pi_{2},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})
such that

\lambda_{1}\leqslant\lambda_{2}\leqslant\lambda,\quad\pi_{1}\leqslant\pi_{2}\leqslant\pi,\quad\pi_{2}\leqslant 2\pi_{1},\quad\pi\leqslant 2\pi_{2},\quad\pi_{2}\leqslant\lambda_{1},\quad\pi\leqslant\lambda_{2},

and

(59)

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\pi_{2}+(1-\pi_{2})h\left(\frac{\pi_{1}-\pi_{2}/2}{1-\pi_{2}}\right),

(60)

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\omega+(1-\omega)h\left(\frac{\pi_{2}-\omega/2}{1-\omega}\right)

The expected number of parity-checks computed is given by
2^{n\>\left(\nu_{3}+o(1)\right)}

Appendix C
Proof of Proposition
4

Let us recall Proposition
4
.
See
4

Let us devise a more convenient expression for
\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle
. By noticing that
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}
and
{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}
are linearly linked we get the following lemma.

Lemma 4
.

Let
{\mathscr{P}}
and
{\mathscr{N}}
be two complementary subsets of
\llbracket 1,n\rrbracket
of size
s
and
n-s
respectively. Let
\mathcal{C}
and
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
be two
[n,k]
linear and
[s,{k_{\textup{aux}}}]
linear codes respectively such that
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathscr{P}}}
is of dimension
s
. Let
{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}
and
\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}
where recall that

\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\times{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\>:|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}|=w\mbox{ and }|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{{\mathbf{c}}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}|={t_{\textup{aux}}}\}.

We have that

(61)

\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle=\langle\left({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle

where
{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s\times(n-s)}
is (independently of the parity-check
\mathbf{h}
) such that

(62)

{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}={\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\intercal}.

Proof.

First, let us show Equation (
62
). Suppose without loss of generality that
{\mathscr{P}}=\llbracket 1,s\rrbracket
and
{\mathscr{N}}=\llbracket s+1,n\rrbracket
. Let
{\mathbf{G}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k\times n}
be a generator matrix of
\mathcal{C}
. Because
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathscr{P}}}
is of dimension
s
there exists an invertible
{\mathbf{J}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k\times k}
such that

{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{G}}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{Id}_{s}&{\mathbf{R}}\\
\mathbf{0}_{k-s}&{\mathbf{R}}^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

where
{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s\times(n-s)}
and
{\mathbf{R}}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{(k-s)\times(n-s)}
. Furthermore,
{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{G}}
is another generator matrix for
\mathcal{C}
. Therefore for any
{\mathbf{h}}\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}
we have
{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{G}}\>{\mathbf{h}}^{\intercal}=\mathbf{0}
. Since
{\mathbf{J}}{\mathbf{G}}{\mathbf{h}}^{\intercal}={\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}^{\intercal}+{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}^{\intercal}
, this gives (
62
).
Now, let us prove (
61
).
Recall that, using Equation (
62
) we have:

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\top}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}{\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle,

and

\displaystyle\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{R}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle

where in the last equality we used Equation (
62
).
This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4
.

Let us consider
{\mathbf{R}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s\times(n-s)}
as in Lemma
4
and let us prove Equation (
12
).
By definition of the bias and
\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}
given Equation (
62
) we have the following computation and ,

\displaystyle\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}

\displaystyle\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\sum_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}(-1)^{\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\left(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\right)_{{\mathscr{N}}}\times{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\\
\left|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\right|=w,\left|{\mathbf{R}}{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right|={t_{\textup{aux}}}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\langle\left({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\intercal}+{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle}

where in the last equality we used Lemma
4
. Therefore,

(63)

\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}=\frac{1}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\left(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\right)_{{\mathscr{N}}}\times{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\end{subarray}}f({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}})

where,

f({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}(-1)^{\langle\left({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\intercal}+{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle}\mathbf{1}_{\{\left|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\right|=w,\left|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}{\mathbf{R}}^{\intercal}+{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right|={t_{\textup{aux}}}\}}.

Using Equation
3
we have that
\left(\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\right)_{{\mathscr{N}}}=\left(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)^{\perp}
and thus
\left(\left(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)^{\perp}\times{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)^{\perp}=\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\times{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}
. By using the Poisson formula
(see
[
MS86
, Lemma 2, Ch. 5.2]
), together with the fact that
\dim\left(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\times{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)=k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}
,
we get

(64)

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\left(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)^{\perp}\times{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\end{subarray}}f({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\left({\mathbf{c}}^{{\mathscr{N}}},{{\mathbf{c}}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\times{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}\end{subarray}}\widehat{f}({\mathbf{c}}^{{\mathscr{N}}},{{\mathbf{c}}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}).

Let us compute the right-hand term. By definition of
f
, it is readily seen that

\displaystyle\widehat{f}(\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n-1},\mathbf{z}_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\\
|\mathbf{z}_{1}|=w,|\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{R}^{\top}+\mathbf{z}_{2}|={t_{\textup{aux}}}\end{subarray}}(-1)^{\langle\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{z}_{1}\rangle+\langle\mathbf{y}_{2},\mathbf{z}_{2}\rangle}(-1)^{\langle\left({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},\mathbf{z}_{1}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},\mathbf{z}_{1}{\mathbf{R}}^{\intercal}+\mathbf{z}_{2}\rangle}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}_{1}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n-s}:|\mathbf{z}_{1}|=w}(-1)^{\langle\mathbf{y}_{1}+\left({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}+\mathbf{y}_{2}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},\mathbf{z}_{1}\rangle}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{z}_{2}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}:|\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{R}^{\intercal}+\mathbf{z}_{2}\end{subarray}|={t_{\textup{aux}}}}(-1)^{\langle\mathbf{y}_{2}+\mathbf{x},\mathbf{z}_{1}\mathbf{R}^{\intercal}+\mathbf{z}_{2}\rangle}

\displaystyle=K^{(n-s)}_{w}\left(\left|\mathbf{y}_{1}+(\mathbf{y}_{2}+\mathbf{x}+\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{P}}})\mathbf{R}+\mathbf{e}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\right|\right)\;K^{(s)}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|\mathbf{y}_{2}+\mathbf{x}\right|\right)

where in the last equality we used Fact
1
. Plugging this into Equation (
64
) and then into Equation (
63
) concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Proposition
5

The proof of this Appendix is to prove Proposition
5
which we recall is given by
See
5

The proof is divided in the following steps.

Step 1:
in Lemma
5
we show that the expected size of
{\mathscr{S}}
is related to the probability that the bias of
\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle
is superior to the threshold
\approx\frac{\delta}{2}
.

Step 2:
We give an exponential bound on the aforementioned probability by using Poisson summation formula as it was done in the proof of Proposition
4
.

Step 1

Recall that we have from Equation
15
that

{\mathscr{S}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\textup{aux}}}\>:\widehat{f_{{\mathbf{y}},\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}},{{\mathbf{G}}_{\textup{aux}}}}}\left({\mathbf{s}}\right)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\>\widetilde{H}\right\}.

By using Lemma
1
we get the following condition for an element
{\mathbf{s}}
to be a candidate:

Fact 4
.

Let
{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}
and
{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}
such that
{\mathbf{x}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}={\mathbf{s}}
. We have,

{\mathbf{s}}\in{\mathscr{S}}\;\Longleftrightarrow\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\;\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}.

From there, we can derive the following lemma linking the expected size of
{\mathscr{S}}
and the previous bias.

Lemma 5
.

Under Distribution
1
, using notation of Proposition
5
and under the constraint of Proposition
5
that
{\mathscr{H}}=\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}
we have

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\;\left(\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|\right)\leqslant 2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\bias_{({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\;\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)+1

where
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
.

Proof.

We have the following computation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathbf{s}}\in{\mathscr{S}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\>:{\mathbf{s}}\neq{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathbf{s}}\in{\mathscr{S}}}\right)

\displaystyle=1+\mathbb{E}\left(\sum_{{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\>:{\mathbf{s}}\neq{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}}\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\sum_{{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\>:{\mathbf{z}}\>{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}={\mathbf{s}}}\mathbf{1}_{{\mathbf{z}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}\in{\mathscr{S}}}\right)

where in the last equality we used that
{{\mathbf{G}}_{\textup{aux}}}
, which is a generator matrix of
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
, has rank
s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}
according to Distribution
1
.
Now, from the linearity of the expectation we get,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant 1+\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\sum_{{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\>:{\mathbf{z}}\notin{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left({\mathbf{z}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}\in{\mathscr{S}}\right)

\displaystyle=1+2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\>\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left({\mathbf{x}}{{\mathbf{G}}^{\intercal}_{\textup{aux}}}\in{\mathscr{S}}\right)

where in the last line we used that
{{\mathbf{G}}_{\textup{aux}}}
has full rank,
\left|{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right|=2^{s}
and
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
. Using Fact
4
concludes the proof.
∎

Step 2

The following lemma relates the upper-bound given in Lemma
5
to the involved probability in Conjecture
1
.

Lemma 6
.

Using Distribution
1
and notation of Proposition
5
we have

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)=\\
\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\>K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right).

where
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
.

Proof.

Recall that we have that from notation of Proposition
5
,

(65)

\widetilde{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}},\qquad\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}(u)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(t-u)}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

According to Proposition
4
we have the following computation,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}

\displaystyle\left(\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\frac{1}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{n-s}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{n-s}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{2}\right)

where in the last equality we used Equation (
65
). It concludes the proof.
∎

We will now use Conjecture
1
to bound the right-hand term of Lemma
6
by the probability of the event “
N_{i,j}\neq 0
” for some low
i
and
j
(more precisely when
K_{w}^{(n-s)}(u)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(t-u)\leqslant K_{w}^{(n-s)}(i)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(j)
).

Lemma 7
.

Under Distribution
1
we have that for
(i,j)\in\llbracket 0,n-s\rrbracket\times\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket
,

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{i,j}\neq 0\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(\frac{\binom{s}{j}\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}+n-k}}\right)

where
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
.

Proof.

This is proved in the first lemma of Appendix
E
.
∎

We are now ready to prove Proposition
5
.

proof of Proposition
5
.

Recall that we want to show,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\binom{s}{j}\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\right)+1.

Lemma
5
gives us that

(66)

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|\right)\leqslant 2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\bias_{({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\;\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)+1

Now using Lemma
6
we get that

(67)

\mathbb{P}\left(\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{\mathbf{y}},{\mathbf{h}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{x}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\rangle\right)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\;\frac{\widetilde{H}}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\right|}\right)=\\
\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\>K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right).

From Conjecture
1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\>K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{i,j}\neq 0\right)\right)

Therefore, using Lemma
7
we get

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}\>K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\frac{\binom{s}{j}\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}+n-k}}\right)

Plugging this into Equation (
67
) and then in (
66
) concludes the proof.
which proves our result.
∎

Appendix E
About the distribution of the
N_{i,j}

This appendix is dedicated to studying the distribution of
N_{i,j}
and in particular to prove Lemma
7
which was used in Appendix
C
to prove Proposition
4
. We recall that it is given by
See
7

First, using the union bound we can devise the following upper bound on our target probability:

Fact 5
.

\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i,j}\neq 0\right)\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left(N_{i,j}\right).

To compute this expected value, we first need to give the following lemma giving the probability that a word belongs to a random code.

Lemma 8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be chosen uniformly at random among the
[n,k]
linear codes. Let
{\mathbf{c}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n}\setminus\{\mathbf{0}\}
we have

(68)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}}\left({\mathbf{c}}\in\mathcal{C}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{2^{k}-1}{2^{n}-1},

(69)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(\mathbf{0}\in\mathcal{C}\right)

\displaystyle=1.

Proof.

This lemma directly follows from
[
BCN89
, §3, Lemma 9.3.2, (iii)]
.
∎

We now give the preliminary lemma which breaks down the expected value of
N_{i,j}
on the
{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}
part and on the
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}
part.

Lemma 9
.

Under Distribution
1
we denote by

\displaystyle\overline{N_{j}^{({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)\right)

where
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
and
\mathbf{r}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+\mathbf{x}
. We denote by fixing
\mathbf{x}
,

(70)

\displaystyle\overline{N_{i}^{(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(N_{i}\left(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}+\left({\mathbf{r}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)\right)

We have that

(71)

\displaystyle\overline{N_{i}^{(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}})}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\left(1+\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-(s-k)}\right)\right)

(72)

\displaystyle\overline{N_{j}^{({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\left(1+\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-\left(s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}\right)}\right)\right)

Furthermore, the term
\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-(s-{k_{\textup{aux}}})}\right)
in Equation (
71
) does not depend on
i
·

Proof.

Under Distribution
1
,
\mathcal{C}
is taken at random among the
[n,k]
-codes that are such that
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathscr{P}}}
is of full rank dimension
s
. Therefore, it is the same as if
\mathcal{C}
was chosen by taking its generator matrix
{\mathbf{G}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k\times n}
as follows:

{\mathbf{G}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{I}}_{s}\\
\mathbf{0}_{k-s}\end{pmatrix},\qquad{\mathbf{G}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\begin{pmatrix}{\mathbf{R}}\\
{\mathbf{G}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\end{pmatrix}.

where
{\mathbf{R}}
is chosen uniformly at random among matrices of
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s\times(n-s)}
and
{\mathbf{G}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}
is any generator matrix of a code chosen uniformly at random among the
[n-s,k-s]
-codes. In particular,
{\mathbf{G}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}
and
\mathbf{R}
are independent.

Now, by definition
{\mathbf{x}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
and
\mathbf{r}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+\mathbf{x}
, therefore
{\mathbf{r}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\neq\mathbf{0}
. We deduce that,
\left({\mathbf{r}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}
is uniformly distributed in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{n-s}
as a non-zero sum of uniformly distribution vectors.
To simplify the notations let us define the uniformly distributed vector

{\mathbf{v}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left({\mathbf{r}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}

which is independent of
\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}
(by construction
{\mathbf{G}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}
and
\mathbf{R}
are independent). Now, let us show Equation (
71
). We have by linearity of the expected value that

\displaystyle\overline{N_{i}^{(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}})}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}^{n-s}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathbf{v}}}\left({\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}+{\mathbf{v}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}^{n-s}}\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathbf{v}}}\left({\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}+{\mathbf{v}}\middle|{\mathbf{v}}={\mathbf{z}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathbf{v}}}\left({\mathbf{v}}={\mathbf{z}}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathbf{v}}}\left({\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}+{\mathbf{v}}\middle|{\mathbf{v}}\neq{\mathbf{z}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathbf{v}}}\left({\mathbf{v}}\neq{\mathbf{z}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{\mathbf{z}}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}^{n-s}}\frac{1}{2^{n-s}}+\frac{2^{k-s}-1}{2^{n-s}-1}\left(1-\frac{1}{2^{n-s}}\right)\qquad\mbox{(Lemma \ref{lem:probaRandom})}

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\left(\frac{2^{n-k}}{2^{n-s}}+2^{n-k}\;\frac{2^{k-s}-1}{2^{n-s}-1}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k-s}}+\frac{2^{-s}-2^{-k}}{2^{-s}-2^{-n}}\;\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\left(\frac{1}{2^{k-s}}+\frac{1-2^{-k+s}}{1-2^{-n+s}}\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\left(1+\frac{1}{2^{k-s}}+\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-k+s}\right)\right)\quad\mbox{($s\leqslant k\leqslant n$)}

Let us now show Equation (
72
). Recall that

\overline{N_{j}^{({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)\right)

where
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
. By definition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s}}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s},\mathbf{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}}\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{x}_{0}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{x},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{x}_{0}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s}}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}}\frac{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}-1}{2^{s}-1}\;\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{x},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{x}_{0}\right)+\mathbb{P}_{\mathbf{x},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{z}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\binom{s}{j}\frac{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}-1}{2^{s}-1}+\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{s}-2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\;2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\;\frac{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}-1}{2^{s}-1}+\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\;\frac{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{s}-2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\;\frac{2^{s}-2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{s}-1}+\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\;\frac{1}{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}-1}

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}\;\left(1+\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}-s}\right)\right)

which completes the proof.
∎

We can now show that the expected value of
N_{i,j}
is the product of the two previously computed quantities:

Lemma 10
.

Under Distribution
1
and when
{\mathbf{x}}
is taken uniformly at random in
\mathbb{F}_{2}^{s}\setminus\{{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\}
we have

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{i,j}\right)=\overline{N_{j}^{({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})}}\;\overline{N_{i}^{(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}})}}

where,

N_{i,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left|\left\{\left({\mathbf{r}},{\mathbf{c}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)\in({\mathbf{x}}+{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})\times\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\>:\left|{\mathbf{r}}\right|=j\mbox{ and }\left|\left({\mathbf{r}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+{\mathbf{c}}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right|=i\right\}\right|

and,

\overline{N_{j}^{({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)\right)\quad\mbox{;}\quad\overline{N_{i}^{(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}})}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C}}\left(N_{i}\left(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}+\left({\mathbf{r}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)\right).

Proof.

By definition,

N_{i,j}=\sum_{u=0}^{N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)}N_{i}\left(\left({\mathbf{r}}^{(u)}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)

where
{\mathbf{r}}^{(u)}
is the
u
’th codeword of weight
j
of
{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}
and
N_{j}({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}})
counts the number of elements in
{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}
of Hamming weight
j
. Therefore,

\displaystyle N_{i,j}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s}}N_{i}\left(\left(\mathbf{z}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)\;\mathds{1}_{``\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime\prime}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s},\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}^{n-s}}\mathds{1}_{``\mathbf{w}\in\left(\mathbf{z}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}"}\;\mathds{1}_{``\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}^{\prime\prime}}

We deduce that,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{i,j}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s},\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}^{n-s}}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{w}\in\left(\mathbf{z}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}},\;\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s},\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}^{n-s}}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{w}\in\left(\mathbf{z}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\mid\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s}}\left(\sum_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{S}_{i}^{n-s}}\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{w}\in\left(\mathbf{z}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\mid\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)\right)\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{z}\in\mathcal{S}_{j}^{s}}\overline{N_{i}^{(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}})}}\;\mathbb{P}\left(\mathbf{z}\in{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)

\displaystyle=\overline{N_{i}^{(\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}})}}\;\overline{N_{j}^{({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}})}}

which completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Lemma
7
.

We prove our result by using Fact
5
, then Lemmas
9
and
10
.
∎

Appendix F
Proof of Proposition
6

F.1.
A more minimalistic conjecture

The goal of this section is to devise a more minimalistic but stronger conjecture which implies Conjecture
1
(in the sense that it involves only concentration bound of the weight enumerator of some random linear codes). Furthermore, the aforementioned implication is a key step of the proof of Proposition
6
which shows that the experimental model implies Conjecture
1
.

Conjecture 2
.

For any
(i,j)\in\llbracket 0,n-s\rrbracket\times\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket
and any
v\in\left\llbracket 0,\overline{V_{j}}+n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},1\right)\right\rrbracket
, we have under Distribution
1
,

(73)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\left|V_{j}-\overline{V_{j}}\right|>n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right),

(74)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-v\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|>n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{v\>\overline{N_{i}}},\>1\right)\middle|\>N_{j}=v\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right).

where

V_{j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}N_{j}\left({\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}\right)

and

(75)

\displaystyle\overline{N_{i}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}},\quad\overline{V_{j}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

Remark 6
.

Recall that
N_{i,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\sum_{u=1}^{V_{j}}N_{i}^{(u)}
where
N^{(u)}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}N_{i}\left(\left({\mathbf{r}}^{(u)}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\right){\mathbf{R}}+{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}+\mathcal{C}^{{\mathscr{N}}}\right)
and
\mathbf{r}^{(u)}
is the
u
’th codeword of weight
j
of
{\mathcal{C}^{\perp}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{x}}
. From this and using Lemma
9
it is readily seen that we have that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(N_{i,j}\middle|\>V_{j}=v\right)=v\>\overline{N_{i}}\left(1+o(1)\right).

As such, Equation (
74
) in the previous Conjecture can also really be seen as a concentration inequality.

Proposition 12
.

Conjecture
2
implies Conjecture
1
.

The following lemmas will be useful to prove the this proposition.

Lemma 11

(Centering Lemma.)
.

We have,

\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)\left(N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right).

Proof.

From the orthogonality of Krawtchouk polynomials relatively to the measure
\mu\left(i\right)=\binom{n-s}{i}

[
MS86
, Ch. 5. §7. Theorem 16]
we have:

\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}\binom{n-s}{i}K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)=0.

By using the definition of
\overline{N_{i}}
in Equation (
75
), we get,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{s}V_{j}\>K^{(s)}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(j\right)\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K^{(n-s)}_{w}\left(i\right)\overline{N_{i}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K^{(n-s)}_{w}\left(i\right)K^{(s)}_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(j\right)V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}.

Therefore,

\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}=\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)\left(N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right)

which completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 2
.

We have,

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)=\\
\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i,j)\in\llbracket 0,n-s\rrbracket\times\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant\frac{1}{2\>(n+1)^{2}}\left|\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right|\right)\right)

Proof.

The event

\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{n-s}\left(i\right)\left(N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right)\geqslant\frac{1}{2}K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)

implies that it exists
j\in\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket
and
i\in\llbracket 0,n-s\rrbracket
such that

(76)

K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{n-s}\left(i\right)\left(N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right)\geqslant\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{2\left(n-s+1\right)\left(s+1\right)}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{n-s}\left(i\right)\left(N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right)\geqslant\frac{1}{2}K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)\quad(\mbox{Lemma }\ref{lem:centering})

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\bigvee_{j=0}^{s}\bigvee_{i=0}^{n-s}\left(K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{n-s}\left(i\right)\left(N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right)\geqslant\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{2\left(n-s+1\right)\left(s+1\right)}\right)\right)\quad(\mbox{using }\eqref{eq:proof_bias_to_NI_doubleRLPN})

\displaystyle\leqslant\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}\mathbb{P}\left(K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{n-s}\left(i\right)\left(N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right)\geqslant\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{2\left(n-s+1\right)\left(s+1\right)}\right)\quad(\mbox{union bound})

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{2}\right)\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=0\dots n-s\\
j=0\dots s\end{subarray}}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant\frac{1}{2\>(n+1)^{2}}\left|\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right|\right)

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 12
.

Under Parameters constraint
1
we have that

\left(\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right)^{2}=\omega\left(n^{\alpha+8}\right)\overline{N_{i}}\max\left(\overline{V_{j}},\frac{1}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)}\right)

Proof.

First, we simplify
\left(K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)^{2}
by using Constraint
(i)

of Parameters constraint
1
which we recall is given by:

\frac{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}=\frac{\omega\left(n^{\alpha+8}\right)}{\delta^{2}},\quad\mbox{where }\;\delta\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{\binom{n-s}{w}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

By reordering the terms in the previous equation we get:

\displaystyle\left(K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)^{2}=2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}\binom{n-s}{w}\omega(n^{\alpha+8}).

Now, to show the lemma we only have to show that

(77)

\displaystyle\frac{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}\binom{n-s}{w}}{\left(K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)\right)^{2}})\geqslant\overline{N_{i}}\max\left(\overline{V_{j}},\frac{1}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)}\right).

First ,let us lower bound
\frac{1}{\left(K_{w}^{(n-s)}(i)\right)^{2}}
. From the orthonormality relations of the Krawtchouk polynomials
[
MS86
, Ch. 5. §7. Theorem 16]
with the measure
\mu_{1}\left(v\right)=\frac{\binom{n-s}{v}}{2^{n-s}}
we have that

\displaystyle\sum_{v=0}^{n-s}\binom{n-s}{v}\left(K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(v\right)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\binom{n-s}{w}2^{n-s},

and thus, as the previous sum is composed of positive terms, we have that

(78)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(K_{w}^{(n-s)}(i)\right)^{2}}\geqslant\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{\binom{n-s}{w}2^{n-s}}.

Now, let us lower-bound
\frac{1}{\left(K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(j)\right)^{2}}
. Using the same orthonormality argument relatively to the measure
\mu_{2}\left(v\right)=\frac{\binom{s}{v}}{2^{s}}
we get

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(j)\right)^{2}}\geqslant\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}2^{s}}.

Furthermore, from Fact
1
we can also deduce the following inequality

\left(K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)\right)^{2}\leqslant\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{2}.

Combining the last two equations we get that

(79)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}(j)\right)^{2}}\geqslant\min\left(\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}2^{s}},\frac{1}{\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{2}}\right).

Finally let us show Equation (
77
) by using Equation (
78
) and (
79
) :

\displaystyle\frac{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}\binom{n-s}{w}}{\left(K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)\right)^{2}}

\displaystyle\geqslant 2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}\binom{n-s}{w}\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{\binom{n-s}{w}2^{n-s}}\>\min\left(\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}2^{s}},\frac{1}{\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\min\left(\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}},\quad\frac{2^{s-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}{\binom{s}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\binom{n-s}{i}}{2^{n-k}}\min\left(\frac{\binom{s}{j}}{2^{{k_{\textup{aux}}}}},\frac{1}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)}\right)\quad\mbox{(Constraint $(iii)$ of Eq. \eqref{constraints:constraint})}

\displaystyle=\overline{N_{i}}\min\left(\overline{V_{j}},\frac{1}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)}\right).

This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
12
.

Let us suppose that the Parameter constraint
1
is verified and that Conjecture
2
is true. We want to show Conjecture
1
holds, namely that

\mathbb{P}\left(\sum_{j=0}^{s}\sum_{i=0}^{n-s}K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)N_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{1}{2}K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)\right)\\
=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i,j}\neq 0\right)+2^{-n}\right).

Using Corollary
2
, we only have to show that for any
(i,j)\in\llbracket 0,n-s\rrbracket\times\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket
we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant\frac{1}{2\>(n+1)^{2}}\left|\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right|\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i^{*},j^{*})\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i^{*},j^{*}}\neq 0\right)+2^{-n}\right).

To ease up the notations let us denote by

(80)

R_{i,j}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{1}{2\>(n+1)^{2}}\left|\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right|.

Thus, we only have to show that

(81)

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant R_{i,j}\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i^{*},j^{*})\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i^{*},j^{*}}\neq 0\right)+2^{-n}\right).

We prove the previous equality for each cases:
(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}
or
(i,j)\notin\mathcal{A}
, where recall that

\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\left(i,j\right)\in\llbracket 0,n-s\rrbracket\times\llbracket 0,s\rrbracket,\;\left|\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right|\leqslant n^{3.2}\right\}.

Cases 1:
Here we suppose that
(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}
. Let us prove Equation (
81
). Using the law of total probability we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant R_{i,j}\right)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i,j}\neq 0\right)+\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant R_{i,j},\;N_{i,j}=0\right).

As
(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}
,

\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i,j}\neq 0\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i^{*},j^{*})\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i^{*},j^{*}}\neq 0\right)\right)

we only have left to show that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant R_{i,j},\>\;N_{i,j}=0\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i^{*},j^{*})\in\mathcal{A}}\mathbb{P}\left(N_{i^{*},j^{*}}\neq 0\right)+2^{-n}\right).

We now show the previous equation, by proving that,

(82)

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant R_{i,j},\>\;N_{i,j}=0\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right).

We have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant R_{i,j},\>\;N_{i,j}=0\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\geqslant R_{i,j}\right)\quad\mbox{(we used that $V_{j},\overline{N_{i}}>0$)}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(V_{j}\geqslant\frac{R_{i,j}}{\overline{N_{i}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(V_{j}-\overline{V_{j}}\geqslant\frac{R_{i,j}}{\overline{N_{i}}}-\overline{V_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(\left|V_{j}-\overline{V_{j}}\right|\geqslant\frac{R_{i,j}}{\overline{N_{i}}}-\overline{V_{j}}\right).

Recall that from Equation (
73
) of Conjecture
2
we have that

\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\left|V_{j}-\overline{V_{j}}\right|>n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},1\right)\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right).

Therefore, we only have to show that for
n
big enough we have

(83)

\frac{R_{i,j}}{\overline{N_{i}}}-\overline{V_{j}}\geqslant n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}},\>1}\right)

to prove Equation (
82
). Let us prove Equation (
83
). By definition of
R_{i,j}
in Equation (
80
) and using Lemma
12
we have that

\displaystyle R_{i,j}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\>(n+1)^{4}}\left(\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\>(n+1)^{4}}\;\omega\left(n^{\alpha+8}\right)\overline{N_{i}}\max\left(\overline{V_{j}},\frac{1}{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha}\right)}\right)

(84)

\displaystyle=f(n)\max\left(\overline{N_{i}}\>\overline{V_{j}},n^{-\alpha}\overline{N_{i}}\right)

where
f(n)=\omega\left(n^{\alpha+4}\right)
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle\frac{R_{i,j}^{2}}{\overline{N_{i}}^{2}}\;\frac{1}{n^{2.4}\max\left(\overline{V_{j}}^{2},\>1\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{f(n)\max\left(\overline{N_{i}}\>\overline{V_{j}},n^{-\alpha}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right)}{n^{2.4}\;\overline{N_{i}}^{2}\max\left(\overline{V_{j}}^{2},\>1\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{f(n)\max\left(\frac{\overline{V_{j}}}{\overline{N_{i}}},\frac{n^{-\alpha}}{\overline{N_{i}}}\>\right)}{n^{2.4}\>\max\left(\overline{V_{j}}^{2},\>1\right)}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n^{2.4}}f(n)\max\left(\frac{1}{\overline{N_{i}}\overline{V_{j}}},\frac{n^{-\alpha}}{\overline{N_{i}}\overline{V_{j}}^{2}}\>\right)&\mbox{ if }\overline{V_{j}}>1\\
\frac{1}{n^{2.4}}f(n)\max\left(\frac{\overline{V_{j}}}{\overline{N_{i}}},\frac{n^{-\alpha}}{\overline{N_{i}}}\>\right)&\mbox{ if }\overline{V_{j}}\leqslant 1\end{cases}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{1}{n^{2.4}}f(n)\min\left(\frac{1}{\overline{N_{i}}\overline{V_{j}}},\frac{n^{-\alpha}}{\overline{N_{i}}}\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{n^{-2\alpha}}{n^{2.4}}\;f(n)\;\min\left(\frac{1}{\overline{N_{i}}\overline{V_{j}}},\frac{1}{n^{-\alpha}\>\overline{N_{i}}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{-2\alpha}}{n^{2.4}}\;\frac{f(n)}{\max\left(\overline{N_{i}}\overline{V_{j}},n^{-\alpha}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{n^{-2\alpha}}{n^{2.4}}\;\frac{f(n)^{2}}{R_{i,j}^{2}}\qquad(\mbox{By Equation \eqref{eq:fn}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\omega\left(n^{5.6}\right)}{R_{i,j}^{2}}\qquad(f(n)=\omega\left(n^{\alpha+4}\right))

(85)

\displaystyle=\omega(1)

where in the last line we used the fact that
(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}
: by definition,

R_{i,j}=\frac{1}{2\>(n+1)^{2}}\;\left|\frac{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(u\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(t-u\right)}{K_{w}^{(n-s)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}^{(s)}\left(j\right)}\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{2\>(n+1)^{2}}\;n^{3.2}

and thus

\displaystyle\frac{1}{R_{i,j}^{2}}

\displaystyle\geqslant\frac{2\>\left(n+1\right)^{2}}{n^{3.2}}.

Finally, Equation (
85
) shows that for
n
big enough

\frac{R_{i,j}}{\overline{N_{i}}}\geqslant n^{1.2}\max\left(\overline{V_{j}},\>1\right)

And as such, for
n
big enough

\displaystyle\frac{R_{i,j}}{\overline{N_{i}}}-\overline{V_{j}}

\displaystyle\geqslant n^{1.1}\max\left(\overline{V_{j}},\>1\right)

\displaystyle\geqslant n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},\>1\right)

which proves Equation (
83
). Therefore we have just proved Equation (
81
) in the case where
(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}
.

Case 2:
. Here we suppose that
(i,j)\notin\mathcal{A}
. Let us prove Equation (
81
).
We only have to prove that:

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|\geqslant R_{i,j}\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right).

Let
M
be defined as

(86)

M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\overline{V_{j}}+n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},1\right).

By the law of total probability we have that

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right|>R_{i,j}\right)=\\
\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right|>R_{i,j}|V_{j}>M\right)\mathbb{P}\left(V_{j}>M\right)+\\
\sum_{v=0}^{M}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right|>R_{i,j}|V_{j}=v\right)\mathbb{P}\left(V_{j}=v\right).

Which we can upper bound by

\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right|>R_{i,j}\right)\leqslant\mathbb{P}\left(V_{j}>M\right)+\max_{v=0...M}\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-v\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|>R_{i,j}|V_{j}=v\right).

By definition of
M
in Equation (
86
) and using Equation (
73
) of Conjecture
2
we get that

\mathbb{P}\left(V_{j}>M\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right).

Now, we only have left to prove that for any
v\in\llbracket 0,M\rrbracket
we have

\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-v\;\overline{N_{i}}\right|>R_{i,j}|V_{j}=v\right)=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right).

Let us consider
v\in\llbracket 0,M\rrbracket
. Let us first show that for
n
big enough we have that

(87)

\displaystyle n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{v\>\overline{N_{i}}},\>1\right)\leqslant R_{i,j}.

We have

\displaystyle n^{2.2}\max\left(v\>\overline{N_{i}},\>1\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant n^{2.2}\max\left(M\>\overline{N_{i}},\>1\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant n^{2.2}\max\left(\overline{V_{j}}\>\overline{N_{i}}+n^{1.1}\overline{N_{i}}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},1\right),\>1\right)

(Using Equation (
86
))

\displaystyle\leqslant\max\left(2\>n^{2.2}\>\overline{V_{j}}\>\overline{N_{i}},\;2\>n^{3.3}\overline{N_{i}}\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},\>2\>n^{3.3}\overline{N_{i}},\>n^{2.2}\right)

To show Equation (
87
), ne only have left to prove that, for
n
big enough, each term in the previous maximum is smaller than
R_{i,j}^{2}
.
First let us recall that by definition of
R_{i,j}
in Equation (
80
) and from Lemma
12
,

R_{i,j}^{2}=\max\left(\omega\left(n^{\alpha+4}\right)\overline{N_{i}}\>\overline{V_{j}},\omega\left(n^{4}\right)\overline{N_{i}}\right).

For
n
big enough we have that:

\displaystyle 2\>n^{2.2}\>\overline{V_{j}}\>\overline{N_{i}}

\displaystyle\leqslant n^{\alpha+4}\overline{N_{i}}\>\overline{V_{j}}\leqslant R_{i,j}^{2},

\displaystyle 2\>n^{3.3}\overline{N_{i}}\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\begin{cases}n^{4}\overline{N_{i}}\leqslant R_{i,j}^{2}&\mbox{ when }\overline{V_{j}}\leqslant 1\\
n^{\alpha+4}\overline{N_{i}}\>\overline{V_{j}}\leqslant R_{i,j}^{2}&\mbox{ when }\overline{V_{j}}>1\end{cases},

\displaystyle 2\>n^{3.3}\>\overline{N_{i}}

\displaystyle\leqslant n^{4}\overline{N_{i}}\leqslant R_{i,j}^{2},

\displaystyle n^{2.2}

\displaystyle\leqslant R_{i,j}^{2}.

Where in the last equation we used the fact that
(i,j)\notin\mathcal{A}
, thus
R_{i,j}\geqslant\frac{n^{3.2}}{2\left(n+1\right)^{2}}
and thus
R_{i,j}^{2}\geqslant n^{2.3}
for
n
big enough. We have shown that

n^{2.2}\max\left(v\>\overline{N_{i}},\>1\right)\leqslant R_{i,j}^{2}

and thus we have shown Equation (
87
).
Finally we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right|>R_{i,j}|V_{j}=v\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(\mathbb{P}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-V_{j}\overline{N_{i}}\right|>n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{v\>\overline{N_{i}}},\>1\right)|V_{j}=v\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right)

where in the last line we used Equation (
74
) of Conjecture
2
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Lemma 13
.

The Poisson Model
1
imply Conjecture
2
.

Proof.

Let
M
be defined as

(88)

M\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\overline{V_{j}}+n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},1\right)

Recall that to show Conjecture
2
we only have to show that

(89)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\left|V_{j}-\overline{V_{j}}\right|\geqslant n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{\overline{V_{j}}},1\right)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right),

(90)

\displaystyle\forall v\in\llbracket 0,M\rrbracket,\>\;\mathbb{P}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}},{\mathbf{x}}}\left(\left|N_{i,j}-v\>\overline{N_{i}}\right|>n^{1.1}\max\left(\sqrt{v\>\overline{N_{i}}},1\right)\>\middle|\>N_{j}=v\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(2^{-n}\right)

Under the Poisson Model
1
we have that

N_{i,j}\sim\mathrm{Poisson}\left(V_{j}\>\overline{N_{i}}\right),\quad V_{j}\sim\mathrm{Poisson}\left(\overline{V_{j}}\right).

We will use the following fact: when
\mathbf{X}
follows a Poisson distribution of parameter
\lambda
and
g(n)=\omega(n)
, then we have that

(91)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\mathbf{X}-\lambda|>g(n)\max\left(\sqrt{\lambda},1\right)\right)=2^{-\omega(n)}.

Let us prove this claim. It is known
[
Gol17
, Prop 11.15]
that we have the following exponential tail bound for
\mathbf{X}
:

\mathbb{P}\left(|\mathbf{X}-\lambda|>r\right)\leqslant 2\>e^{\frac{-\>r^{2}}{2\>(\lambda+r)}}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(|\mathbf{X}-\lambda|>g(n)\max\left(\sqrt{\lambda},1\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\>e^{\frac{-\>g(n)^{2}\max\left(\lambda,1\right)}{2\>(\lambda+g(n)\max\left(\sqrt{\lambda},1\right))}}

\displaystyle\leqslant 2\>e^{\frac{-\>g(n)}{2\left(\frac{\lambda+g(n)\max\left(\sqrt{\lambda},1\right)}{g(n)\max\left(\lambda,1\right)}\right)}}.

We only have left to show that

\frac{\lambda+g(n)\max\left(\sqrt{\lambda},1\right)}{g(n)\max\left(\lambda,1\right)}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right).

First it is readily seen that we have that (
g(n)=\omega(n)
)

\frac{\lambda}{g(n)\max\left(\lambda,1\right)}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right),

and second,

\frac{g(n)\max\left(\sqrt{\lambda},1\right)}{g(n)\max\left(\lambda,1\right)}=\begin{cases}1=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)&\mbox{ if }\lambda\leqslant 1\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{\lambda}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(1\right)&\mbox{ if }\lambda>1.\end{cases}

which concludes the proof of Equation (
91
). Equation (
89
) directly follows from Equation (
91
). Equation (
90
) also directly follow sfrom Equation (
91
) by noticing that
N_{i,j}=v\sim\mathrm{Poisson}\left(v\>\overline{N_{i}}\right)
when
V_{j}=v
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6
.

Apply successively Lemma
13
and Proposition
12
.
∎

Appendix G
Instantiating the Auxiliary Code
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
with an Efficient Decoder

We use here notation from §
7
. In particular, we suppose the auxiliary code
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
is a product of
b
small random codes where

(92)

b=\Theta(\log n).

We have to show that for such
b
, the
analyses
from Propositions
2
and
5
still hold. Indeed, these analyses were done as if
{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}
were a random code equipped with genie aided decoders. Here we compute
{\mathscr{H}}
as a subset of

\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}\subseteq\left\{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right)\in\mathcal{C}^{\perp}\times{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}\>:\>\forall i\in\left\llbracket 1,b\right\rrbracket,\;|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i)|=\tfrac{w}{b}\mbox{ and }|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i)+{\mathbf{c}}_{i}|=\tfrac{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{b}\right\}

by decoding each parity-check performing an exhaustive search on each block. We have therefore to show that in this case,

i.

the bias

\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle\right)

is of the same order as that given by Proposition
2
,

ii.

and the number of candidates to test

\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|\right)

is of the same order as that given by Proposition
5
.

To prove item
i
., we first suppose that
{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}
and
{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}
have a weight which is fairly distributed, that is:

(93)

\forall i\in\left\llbracket 1,b\right\rrbracket,\;\left|{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i)\right|=\tfrac{t-u}{b}\mbox{ and }\left|{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i)\right|=\tfrac{u}{b}.

This happens with a probability:

(94)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{succ}}=\dfrac{\binom{s/b}{(t-u)/b}^{b}\binom{(n-s)/b}{u/b}^{b}}{\binom{s}{t-u}\binom{n-s}{u}}

\displaystyle=\Omega\left(n\left(\tfrac{b}{cn}\right)^{b}\right)

where
c
is constant in
n
.
So we only need to iterate the whole double-RLPN algorithm a sub-exponential number of times, namely at most
\tfrac{1}{\mathbb{P}_{\mathrm{succ}}}
times. Note that (
93
) is not a necessary condition to achieve our decoding so this overcost is overestimated.

Now, assuming Condition (
93
), then we can see the bias above as the product of
b
independent biases involving smaller vectors. More formally, we have

(95)

\begin{array}[]{l}\displaystyle{\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}},{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\overline{{\mathscr{H}}}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}},{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}},{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}\rangle\right)}\\
\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ =\displaystyle{\prod_{i=1}^{b}\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}}(i),{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}(i)\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}_{i}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}(i)+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i),{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i)\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i),{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i)\rangle\right)}\end{array}

where

(96)
