\begin{array}[]{l}\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}}_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\Big\{\left({\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i),{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i),{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}(i)\right)\in\left(\mathcal{C}_{{\mathscr{P}}(i)\cup{\mathscr{N}}(i)}\right)^{\perp}\times\mathcal{C}_{i}\\
\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ :\;|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i)|=\tfrac{w}{b}\mbox{ and }|{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i)+{\mathbf{c}}_{i}|=\tfrac{{t_{\textup{aux}}}}{b}\Big\}\end{array}

Moreover, let us degrade the Constraints (
7
) of Proposition
2
by replacing the polynomial factor
n^{\alpha}
by a super-polynomial

(97)

A\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{n^{\alpha-1+\log(c)+\log(n)}}{\log(n)^{\log(n)}}.

On the one hand, this super-polynomial factor is multiplied to the final complexity, but on the other hand the new constraint (with the original one (
8
)) induces:

(98)

\displaystyle\frac{\binom{(n-s)/b}{w/b}\binom{s/b}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}/b}}{2^{(k-{k_{\textup{aux}}})/b}}

\displaystyle=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\omega\mathopen{}\left(\frac{n^{\alpha/\log(n)}}{\delta^{2/\log(n)}}\right)

and

(99)

\frac{\binom{(n-s)/b}{w/b}\binom{s/b}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}/b}}{2^{k/b}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha/\log(n)}\right)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\frac{\binom{s/b}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}/b}}{2^{(s-{k_{\textup{aux}}})/b}}=\mathop{}\mathopen{}\mathcal{O}\mathopen{}\left(n^{\alpha/\log(n)}\right).

Which allows us to say, using Proposition
2
, that for all
i\in\left\llbracket 1,b\right\rrbracket
and for a proportion
1-\mathop{}\mathopen{}o\mathopen{}\left(1\right)
of codes
\mathcal{C}_{i}
and
\mathcal{C}_{{\mathscr{P}}(i)\cup{\mathscr{N}}(i)}
:

(100)

\bias_{\left({\mathbf{h}}(i),{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}(i)\right){\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\$}}{{\leftarrow}}}\overline{{\mathscr{H}}}_{i}}\left(\langle{{\mathbf{c}}_{\textup{aux}}}(i)+{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i),{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{P}}}(i)\rangle+\langle{\mathbf{e}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i),{\mathbf{h}}_{{\mathscr{N}}}(i)\rangle\right)=\delta^{1/\log(n)}(1-\mathop{}\mathopen{}o\mathopen{}\left(1\right)).

By specifying the values of both
\mathop{}\mathopen{}o\mathopen{}\left(1\right)
(see proof of Proposition
2
in Appendix
A
), we can deduce that
i
. is verified.

To verify item
ii
., we can adapt Section
D
to show that

(101)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathcal{C},{\mathcal{C}_{\textup{aux}}}}\left(\left|{\mathscr{S}}\right|\right)

\displaystyle=\displaystyle{\mathop{}\mathopen{}\widetilde{\mathcal{O}}\mathopen{}\left(\max_{(i,j)\in\mathcal{A}}\frac{\binom{s/b}{j}^{b}\binom{(n-s)/b}{i}^{b}}{2^{n-k}}\right)+1}

where

(102)

\mathcal{A}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\left\{\left(i,j\right)\in\llbracket 0,\tfrac{n-s}{b}\rrbracket\times\llbracket 0,\tfrac{s}{b}\rrbracket,\frac{K_{w/b}^{((n-s)/b)}\left(u/b\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}/b}^{(s/b)}\left((t-u)/b\right)}{K_{w/b}^{((n-s)/b)}\left(i\right)K_{{t_{\textup{aux}}}/b}^{(s/b)}\left(j\right)}\leqslant n^{2/b}\right\},

(103)

{\mathscr{S}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\{{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathbb{F}_{2}^{k_{\textup{aux}}}\>:\widehat{f_{{\mathbf{y}},\widetilde{{\mathscr{H}}},{{\mathbf{G}}_{\textup{aux}}}}}\left({\mathbf{s}}\right)\geqslant\frac{\delta}{2}\>\widetilde{H}\},

and

(104)

\widetilde{H}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}\frac{\binom{(n-s)/b}{w/b}^{b}\binom{s/b}{{t_{\textup{aux}}}/b}^{b}}{2^{k-{k_{\textup{aux}}}}}.

Finally, up to a sub-exponential factor, the above expectation is of the same order as in Proposition
5
.

Appendix H
Proofs of the statements made in Section
8

Proof of Proposition
7

See
7

It is helpful to notice before the following link between the Bessel functions and the Fourier transform of the indicator function

{\mathbbm{1}}_{\leqslant w}

of the words of Euclidean norm
\leqslant w

in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
(see
[
DDRT23
, Fact 4.11]
)

Lemma 14
.

We have for any positive integer
n
, any
w\geqslant 0
, any
{\mathbf{x}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{n}

\widehat{{\mathbbm{1}}_{\leqslant w}}({\mathbf{x}})=\left(\frac{w}{\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}}\right)^{n/2}J_{n/2}\left(2\pi w\|\mathbf{x}\|_{2}\right)

where
\widehat{f}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{n}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{-2i\pi\langle\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\rangle}d\mathbf{y}
for
f:\mathbb{R}^{n}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
.

Proof of Proposition
7
.

First, notice that,

\displaystyle f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\mathbf{w}\in\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}\left(e^{2i\pi\langle\mathbf{w},\mathbf{y}\rangle}+e^{-2i\pi\langle\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\rangle}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{w}\in\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}e^{-2i\pi\langle\mathbf{w},\mathbf{y}\rangle}\quad(\mbox{$\mathbf{w}\mapsto-\mathbf{w}$ is a bijection in ${\mathscr{W}}$})

(105)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\mathbf{w}\in\Lambda^{\vee}}\left({\mathbbm{1}}_{\leqslant w+\varepsilon}(\mathbf{w})-{\mathbbm{1}}_{\leqslant w-\varepsilon}(\mathbf{w})\right)e^{-2i\pi\langle\mathbf{w},\mathbf{y}\rangle}

Recall now the Poisson summation formula, for any
\mathbf{y}\in\Lambda+\mathbf{e}
and sufficiently regular function
f
,

\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\Lambda^{\vee}}f(\mathbf{x})e^{-2i\pi\langle\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}\rangle}=\frac{1}{|\Lambda^{\vee}|}\;\sum_{\mathbf{x}\in\Lambda+\mathbf{e}}\widehat{f}(\mathbf{x})

Plugging this formula into Equation (
105
) yields to,

\displaystyle f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}({\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda^{\vee}|}\sum_{\mathbf{x\in\Lambda+\mathbf{e}}}\left(\widehat{{\mathbbm{1}}_{\leqslant w+\varepsilon}}(\mathbf{x})-\widehat{{\mathbbm{1}}_{\leqslant w-\varepsilon}}(\mathbf{x})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda^{\vee}|}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\frac{N_{j}}{j^{n/2}}\left((w+\varepsilon)^{n/2}J_{n/2}\left(2\pi(w+\varepsilon)j\right)-(w-\varepsilon)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(2\pi(w-\varepsilon)j)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\Lambda^{\vee}|}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\frac{N_{j}}{(2\pi)^{n/2}\;j^{n}}\Big(\left(2\pi(w+\varepsilon)j\right)^{n/2}J_{n/2}\left(2\pi(w+\varepsilon)j\right)

(106)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-\left(2\pi(w-\varepsilon)j\right)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(2\pi(w-\varepsilon)j)\Big)

which
concludes the proof.
∎

An Approximation.

We have also an approximate form for
f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}({\mathbf{y}})
which is given by

(107)

f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}({\mathbf{y}})\approx\frac{4\pi\varepsilon}{|\Lambda^{\vee}|}\;\sum_{j\geqslant 0}jN_{j}\;\left(\frac{w}{j}\right)^{n/2}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj).

This follows from the fact that

(108)

\frac{d}{dx}\left(x^{n/2}J_{n/2}(x)\right)=x^{n/2}J_{n/2-1}(x).

Let,

X\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}2\pi wj\quad\mbox{ and }\quad h\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textrm{def}}}{{=}}2\pi\varepsilon j

Notice that,

\left(\left(2\pi(w+\varepsilon)j\right)^{n/2}J_{n/2}\left(2\pi(w+\varepsilon)j\right)-\left(2\pi(w-\varepsilon)j\right)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(2\pi(w-\varepsilon)j)\right)\\
=(X+h)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(X+h)-(X-h)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(X-h)

From Equation (
108
),

\displaystyle(X+h)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(X+h)-(X-h)^{n/2}J_{n/2}(X-h)

\displaystyle\approx 2h\;\frac{d}{dX}\left(X^{n/2}J_{n/2}(X)\right)

\displaystyle=2h\;X^{n/2}J_{n/2-1}(X)

\displaystyle=(4\pi\varepsilon j)\;(2\pi wj)^{n/2}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj)

Plugging this into Equation (
106
) yields (
107
).

We also recall that we make the approximation

(109)

f_{{{{\mathscr{W}}}}}({\mathbf{y}})\approx\frac{N}{\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}\right|}\;f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}({\mathbf{y}})

The number
N_{\leqslant x}^{\vee}
of dual vectors of length
\leqslant x
can be approximated using the Gaussian heuristic:

N_{\leqslant x}^{\vee}\approx\frac{x^{n}}{\sqrt{n\pi}\;\left(\frac{n}{2\pi e}\right)^{n/2}}\;\frac{1}{|\Lambda^{\vee}|}

Thus we have:

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}\right|

\displaystyle=N_{\leqslant w+\varepsilon}^{\vee}-N_{\leqslant w-\varepsilon}^{\vee}\approx\frac{(w+\varepsilon)^{n}-(w-\varepsilon)^{n}}{\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{2\pi e}}^{n}\cdot\sqrt{\pi n}\cdot|\Lambda^{\vee}|}\approx\frac{2n\varepsilon w^{n-1}}{\sqrt{\tfrac{n}{2\pi e}}^{n}\;\sqrt{\pi n}\;|\Lambda^{\vee}|}

Putting this into Equation (
109
) shows,

f_{{\mathscr{W}}}(\mathbf{y})\approx N\;\frac{\left|\Lambda^{\vee}\right|\;\sqrt{n\pi}\left(\frac{n}{2\pi e}\right)^{n/2}}{2n\varepsilon w^{n-1}}\;f_{\widetilde{{\mathscr{W}}}}(\mathbf{y})

and after some further computation

\displaystyle f_{{\mathscr{W}}}(\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle\approx N\;\left|\Lambda^{\vee}\right|\frac{\sqrt{n\pi}\left(\frac{n}{2\pi e}\right)^{n/2}}{2n\varepsilon w^{n-1}}\;\frac{4\pi\varepsilon}{|\Lambda^{\vee}|}\;\sum_{j\geqslant 0}jN_{j}\;\left(\frac{w}{j}\right)^{n/2}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj)

\displaystyle=N\;\frac{\sqrt{n\pi}}{2nw^{n-1}}\left(\frac{n}{2\pi e}\right)^{n/2}\;4\pi\sum_{j\geqslant 0}jN_{j}\;\left(\frac{w}{j}\right)^{n/2}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj)

\displaystyle=N\;\frac{\sqrt{n}\;n^{n/2}}{n}\;\frac{1}{w^{n-1}}\;\frac{\sqrt{\pi}\;\pi}{\pi^{n/2}}\;\frac{4}{2\;2^{n/2}}\;\frac{1}{e^{n/2}}\;\sum_{j\geqslant 0}N_{j}\;w^{n/2}\frac{1}{j^{n/2-1}}\;J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj)

\displaystyle=N\;\sqrt{n}\;\sqrt{\pi}\;e^{-1}\;n^{n/2-1}\;\pi^{-(n/2-1)}\;2^{-(n/2-1)}e^{-(n/2-1)}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}N_{j}\left(\frac{1}{wj}\right)^{n/2-1}\;J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj)

\displaystyle=N\frac{\sqrt{n\pi}}{e}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}N_{j}\left(\frac{n}{2\pi ewj}\right)^{n/2-1}J_{n/2-1}(2\pi wj)
